<summary><b>203. Weather Heads-Up Before You Leave</b> —— Sunny morning, no umbrella — afternoon downpour soaks you through…</summary>

Left the house under a clear sky with no umbrella, got drenched in the afternoon storm. Happens several times a year.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "Check the weather every morning at 7:30 — warn me about rain, cold snaps, or smog."

2. The night before a trip, ask it: "Heading to Shanghai tomorrow — what should I wear, do I need an umbrella?" It'll check the local forecast and advise.

3. Got weekend outdoor plans? Have it watch the forecast three days out and tell you immediately if things turn.

**Real talk:** Checking and nudging are all it; but you still have to grab the umbrella yourself. It warned you — getting soaked anyway is on you.

</details>

<a id="skill-204"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>204. Stuck-Package Alerts</b> —— No movement for a week — turns out it's been sitting at a transfer hub for three days…</summary>

Nothing moved for a week. Checked and found it stuck at a transfer hub for three days, then spent half a day arguing with customer service.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. After ordering, send Muse the tracking number: "Watch this package — should arrive in 5 days, ping me if it's late."

2. No update for two days? Screenshot the tracking page and send it over — it'll judge whether it's normal transit or actually stuck.

3. Once it's confirmed stuck, have it draft a nudge you can paste straight to customer service.

**Real talk:** The delivery app gives it no API access, so screenshots are on you. It does the date math, sets the reminders, and reads the screenshots for trouble.

</details>

<a id="skill-205"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>205. Fridge Expiry Watch</b> —— Found yogurt two months past date in the back of the fridge — throwing it out hurt…</summary>

Found yogurt two months expired in the back of the fridge. Throwing it away stung — money down the drain.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. After grocery shopping, snap a photo and tell Muse: "Log these groceries and their expiry dates — warn me before they go bad."

2. It builds the list: milk 7 days, yogurt 14 days, frozen dumplings 3 months — each with its own reminder.

3. Before things expire, ask: "What three dishes can I make with what's about to go off?" Instant recipes.

**Real talk:** You photograph the groceries yourself — it can't see inside your fridge. Lists and recipes are on it; the actual cooking is on you.

</details>

<a id="skill-206"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>206. Kids' Class Reminders</b> —— Five after-school classes a week — piano, English — the whole schedule lives in a parent's head…</summary>

Five after-school classes a week, piano and English alternating — the whole schedule lives in a parent's head.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse a screenshot of the class schedule: "Remind me one hour before each class to get ready."

2. Have it remember what to bring: swim gear for swim class, sketch bag for art — it tells you along with the reminder.

3. When a class moves ("Wednesday piano is now Friday"), tell it and it updates everything automatically.

**Real talk:** You send the screenshot; it does the nagging. Driving the kid and packing the bag? Still parent duty.

</details>

<a id="skill-207"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>207. Pet Vaccine Reminders</b> —— Missed the cat's second combo shot — the vet said the first one no longer counts…</summary>

Missed the cat's second combo shot — the vet said we have to restart the schedule, so the first shot was wasted.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph the vaccine booklet and tell Muse: "Log the shots given and the next due date — remind me 7 days ahead."

2. Log the recurring stuff too — deworming, checkups — and it'll auto-schedule the next round.

3. After each new shot, send the updated record; it keeps watching the next one. No more gaps.

**Real talk:** You photograph the booklet; it does the reminding. Hauling the cat to the vet is still your trip.

</details>

<a id="skill-208"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>208. Water & Power Outage Alerts</b> —— Saw the outage notice after the water was already off — no brushing, no washing, chaos…</summary>

Didn't see the property notice until the water was already off — unbrushed teeth, unwashed face, whole family scrambling.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "Search for upcoming planned water and power outages in my city — tell me if my neighborhood is on the list."

2. Forward screenshots of last-minute notices from the residents' group chat; it'll distill each to one line: when it stops, when it's back, which buildings.

3. Have it put confirmed outages on your calendar and remind you the night before: fill water jugs, charge everything, stock bottled water.

**Real talk:** It can't see your neighborhood-only group chat — forwarding screenshots is on you. It turns them into reminders and calendar entries.

</details>

<a id="skill-209"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>209. Which Bin Does This Go In</b> —— Standing at the bins holding a delivery box — food waste, hazardous, recyclable?…</summary>

Standing at the bins holding a delivery box, blanking on the categories — and getting lectured by the volunteer when you guess wrong.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Snap a photo and ask Muse "which bin does this go in" — instant answer with the reasoning.

2. When unsure why ("why is a milk-tea cup residual waste?"), ask and it'll explain the rule so it sticks.

3. Have it make you a "20 common household items" sorting chart — stick it on the fridge, whole family follows it.

**Real talk:** You take the photo — it can't see the box in your hand. It tells you the bin; hauling the trash downstairs is still you.

</details>

<a id="skill-210"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>210. Flower & Plant ID</b> —— Gorgeous roadside blooms — someone asks what they are and all you've got is "no idea, pretty though"…</summary>

Gorgeous flowers blooming by the roadside — someone asks what they're called and all you can say is "no idea, pretty though."

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Snap a photo and ask Muse "what flower is this" — name, family, and bloom season.

2. Follow up: "Is it easy to grow? Can I keep it on my balcony?" — light, watering, and pitfalls.

3. For favorites, have it keep an archive: "Build me a plant album — where I saw it, what it's called." Over time it becomes your bloom map.

**Real talk:** You take the photos — it can't go outside. It IDs the flowers and keeps the album; buying seedlings and watering them is on you.

</details>

<a id="skill-211"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>211. Medicine Cabinet Expiry Tracker</b> —— Still taking cold remedy two years expired — wouldn't even know if it caused problems.</summary>

Still taking cold remedy two years past its date — wouldn't even know if something went wrong.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph the production and expiry dates on every box at home; have Muse build an expiry list.

2. Tell it: "Warn me when something has 3 months left; flag expired ones in red so I toss them."

3. Before buying new meds, ask: "Do we already have something for this? Don't double-buy."

**Real talk:** It tracks dates — it can't judge whether a pill is still safe. When in doubt, ask a pharmacist.

</details>

<a id="skill-212"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>212. Appliance Repair Without Getting Ripped Off</b> —— AC stopped cooling — the tech wants to replace the motherboard, quote: 800.</summary>

AC stopped cooling. The repair tech shows up and immediately wants to replace the motherboard — quote: 800 yuan.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Describe the symptoms to Muse first: "What's usually wrong here, and what should the repair cost?"

2. Photograph the tech's quote and have it judge: "Is this price inflated?"

3. After the repair, log it: which appliance, when, how much. Next breakdown, you'll know the going rate.

**Real talk:** It can't fix things or haggle for you — the hands-on work and the paying are yours.

</details>

<a id="skill-213"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>213. All Your Pickup Codes on One Screen</b> —— Three packages, three pickup codes — digging through texts while the line builds behind you.</summary>

Three packages, three pickup codes — digging through text messages while the line builds up behind you at the locker station.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Forward all the pickup-code texts to Muse.

2. Tell it: "Make me one table: carrier, code, locker location — ordered along my route."

3. Check each one off as you grab it; it tracks what's left at a glance.

**Real talk:** Organizing is all it; but picking up the packages and reading out the codes is in-person only — it can't run the errand for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-214"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>214. Monthly Utility Bill Reconciliation</b> —— Water, power, gas bills each in their own app — no idea if you spent more this month…</summary>

Water, power, and gas bills each live in their own app — no sense of whether you spent more this month.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse screenshots of each month's utility payments.

2. Tell it: "Build a monthly comparison table — flag which one jumped the most."

3. Three months climbing in a row? Have it nudge you to investigate — could be a leak or standby power drain.

**Real talk:** Tables and detective work are on it, but paying the bills is forever yours — it won't cover your utilities.

</details>

<a id="skill-215"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>215. School Notices in Three Sentences</b> —— 200 messages a day in the class group chat — the announcements drown in stickers.</summary>

200 messages a day in the class group chat — the important notices drown in sticker spam.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Forward or paste the announcements to Muse.

2. Tell it: "Boil this down to three sentences: what's happening, what to bring, what's the deadline."

3. For payments and forms, have it create to-dos and remind you — no more last-day scrambles.

**Real talk:** Distilling and tracking are on it; but paying and filling out forms happens in the group chat, by you — it can't get into your kid's class group.

</details>

<a id="skill-216"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>216. Parents' Medication Chart</b> —— Mom and Dad take seven or eight meds — which go before meals, which after, all from memory.</summary>

Mom and Dad take seven or eight medications — which ones before meals, which after, all held together by memory.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph each medication's instructions or box and send them to Muse.

2. Tell it: "Make a medication chart — name, times per day, before/after meals. Big font, I'm printing it for the fridge."

3. Have it warn you a week before something runs out so there's never a gap.

**Real talk:** It organizes and reminds; changing or adding meds means a doctor visit with your parents — its word doesn't count there.

</details>

<a id="skill-217"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>217. Moving Box Checklist</b> —— Started packing three days before moving day — found the charger left at the old place after.</summary>

Started packing three days before moving day — found the charger left behind at the old place afterward.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "Two-bedroom place, moving next week — give me a packing list by room."

2. As you finish each box, tell it; it numbers them for you: Box 3 = kitchen spices.

3. Unpack against the list at the new place — you'll know instantly which box is missing instead of tearing the place apart.

**Real talk:** Lists and numbering are on it; but packing, hauling, and unpacking are pure sweat equity — it can't lift your wardrobe.

</details>

<a id="skill-218"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>218. Pet Medical Records</b> —— Every new vet means retelling the whole history — and always leaving something out.</summary>

Every new vet means retelling the whole medical history — and always leaving something out.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph past lab results and diagnoses and send them to Muse.

2. Tell it: "Build a pet health record: date, symptoms, diagnosis, meds."

3. Next visit, hand the record to the vet — one glance and they get it, no more charades from you.

**Real talk:** Compiling the record is on it; but you're the one carrying the cat in and talking to the vet — it can't examine your fur baby.

---

## 🛒 Shopping & Saving

> Compare prices, bundle orders, track price drops, audit subscriptions

<a id="skill-245"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>245. Sale Bundle Calculator</b> —— 50-off-300 coupon, cart at 268 — pad it with junk or kiss the 50 goodbye?…</summary>

50-off-300 coupon, cart sitting at 268 — pad it with junk or kiss the 50 goodbye. Half an hour of checkout agony.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse a cart screenshot or item list, plus the coupon threshold and amount.

2. Tell it: "Do the math — what's the cheapest useful add-on to hit the threshold? Lowest real total, and the add-on has to be a daily essential, not junk."

3. Have it lay out a before/after comparison table — order off the cheapest row.

**Real talk:** The math is all its; but placing the order and paying off the cheapest row is your click — it can't pay for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-246"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>246. Price History Lookup</b> —— Headphones at 899 now — were they 699 last month? Terrified of buying the top…</summary>

Headphones at 899 yuan now — were they 699 last month? Terrified of buying at the peak like a sucker.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the product link or name: "Pull this item's price trend for the last three months."

2. Have it mark the historical low and when it hit — is now a good time to buy?

3. If not, tell it: "Ping me if it drops below X" — a scheduled price watch.

**Real talk:** You send the link; price checks and the stakeout are on it. Buy or wait — your call.

</details>

<a id="skill-247"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>247. Post-Purchase Price Protection</b> —— Appliance drops 300 a week after you bought it — 30 days of protection, and you keep forgetting to check…</summary>

Appliance drops 300 yuan a week after you bought it — 30 days of price protection, and you keep meaning to check the order page until it lapses.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. After ordering, screenshot it for Muse: "Log this item and date — check daily during the protection window."

2. If it drops, it tells you exactly how much you'd get back — one tap in the order page to claim.

3. It ends the watch automatically at expiry. Nothing for you to remember.

**Real talk:** The claim tap happens in the app, by you — it watches prices and does the math, but logins and submissions are hands-off for it.

</details>

<a id="skill-248"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>248. Subscription Expiry Reminders</b> —— A pile of memberships — auto-renewal charges hit before you even remember…</summary>

Video, cloud storage, a pile of memberships — auto-renewal charges land before you even remember, non-refundable.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. List every recurring membership for Muse: name, expiry date, monthly cost.

2. Tell it: "Remind me 7 days before each one — ask me if I want to keep it." Calendar entries plus timed nudges.

3. Decide on reminder day: keep it, or open the app and kill auto-renew.

**Real talk:** Muse only reminds — killing auto-renew means opening each app yourself. No permissions for it there.

</details>

<a id="skill-249"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>249. Secondhand Pricing for Your Stuff</b> —— Old camera, old GPU to sell — price high and crickets, price low and you bleed…</summary>

Old camera, old GPU to sell — price high and it's crickets, price low and you bleed. Pure guesswork.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph the item for Muse — brand, model, condition.

2. Tell it: "Check what the same model goes for secondhand — give me a price range."

3. List in that range: floor price if you're in a hurry, ceiling if you can wait for the right buyer.

**Real talk:** Price research is on it; but listing, haggling with buyers, and shipping happen in the app, by you — it can't log into your Xianyu (China's secondhand marketplace).

</details>

<a id="skill-250"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>250. Phone Plan Checkup</b> —— 100+ yuan a month — data unused, minutes untouched. Basically donating to the carrier…</summary>

100+ yuan a month on the phone bill — data unused, minutes untouched. Basically donating to the carrier.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot your plan details and last three months' bills from the carrier app for Muse.

2. Tell it: "Analyze my actual usage — recommend a cheaper plan, calculate the yearly savings."

3. Like its pick? Change the plan in the app yourself — takes effect that month.

**Real talk:** The carrier app has no open API — screenshots and plan changes are hands-on, by you. Muse just does the math and makes the case.

</details>

<a id="skill-251"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>251. Shared Subscriptions Without the Drama</b> —— Splitting memberships with friends — who renews, how to split, endless group-chat debates…</summary>

Splitting video and cloud memberships with friends — who renews, how to split the cost, endless group-chat debates every cycle.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Give Muse the shared memberships, expiry dates, and per-person amounts — it keeps a split sheet.

2. Tell it: "Remind me three days before expiry — and calculate exactly what everyone owes me."

3. Someone leaves or joins? Toss it the new roster — amounts recalculate automatically.

**Real talk:** The platforms give it no API — the renewal tap is yours, and don't share passwords with it. It just keeps the books and the timeline straight.

</details>

<a id="skill-252"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>252. Cross-Border Tax Math</b> —— Looks cheap overseas — add duties and shipping and it's pricier than local. Realized at checkout…</summary>

Looks cheap from overseas — add import duties and shipping and it's pricier than buying local. Realized at checkout. Ouch.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Give Muse the item price, category, and country: "Calculate my all-in total."

2. Tell it: "Break out the duty rate and shipping — no guessing."

3. Compare against the domestic price — buy wherever's cheaper; if the tax is unavoidable, accept it.

**Real talk:** You report price and category; it does all the math. Ordering, paying, and customs clearance are still you.

</details>

<a id="skill-253"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>253. Trade-In vs. Buy New: Appliances</b> —— "500 back for trade-in!" — genuinely cheap, or markup-then-discount?…</summary>

Old AC needs replacing — the platform offers 500 back for trade-in. Genuinely cheap, or markup-then-discount?

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph the old appliance's brand and model for Muse; tell it the new model you want.

2. Tell it: "Find this model's lowest non-promo price, add the trade-in value — which deal actually wins?"

3. Have it build the comparison table: buy outright vs. trade-in — then pull the trigger with eyes open.

**Real talk:** You photograph the old unit; it does the math. Ordering the new one and handing the old one to the recycler are in-person, by you.

</details>

<a id="skill-254"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>254. Group Order Bill Splitting</b> —— Three people splitting snacks — coupons used, shipping split, who owes what?…</summary>

Three people splitting a snack order — coupons used, shipping split, who owes what? Friendship-ending math.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the order screenshot — and who wanted what.

2. Tell it: "Split discounts and shipping proportionally — exact per-person totals, down to the cent."

3. Screenshot its bill into the group chat — nobody overpays a cent.

**Real talk:** You report the order and who-gets-what; it can split hairs on the math, but collecting the money still means speaking up in the group yourself.

</details>

<a id="skill-255"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>255. Livestream Price Check</b> —— Host screams "lowest price on the internet" — you order, then find it 50 cheaper next door.</summary>

Host screams "lowest price on the entire internet" — you order, then find it 50 yuan cheaper on the next platform over.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot the livestream product for Muse.

2. Tell it: "Check what this goes for everywhere — is the stream deal real or markup-then-discount?"

3. Only order if it's genuinely cheap — and have it watch for 7 days, pinging you to claim price protection if it drops.

**Real talk:** Tapping "buy" is yours — it compares prices; it can't out-click you on coupon grabs either.

</details>

<a id="skill-256"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>256. Coupon Expiry Alerts</b> —— Pile of coupons claimed — remembered only after they expired. Tens of yuan wasted.</summary>

Claimed a pile of coupons — remembered only after they expired. Tens of yuan down the drain.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot your coupons from each platform for Muse.

2. Tell it: "Sort by expiry — warn me 3 days before each one dies."

3. When the nudge comes, ask: "What's the smartest buy with this coupon?" Don't force-spend just to use it.

**Real talk:** Sorting and reminding are on it; but opening the app and paying is you — in its hands a coupon is just a string of text.

</details>

<a id="skill-257"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>257. Cooling-Off Period for Impulse Buys</b> —— Late-night scrolling, three impulse orders — morning regret, plus return shipping.</summary>

Late-night scrolling, three impulse orders — morning regret, plus return shipping on top.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Make a deal with Muse: "When I say 'I want to buy' something, park it on a cooling list — remind me in 24 hours."

2. When the timer's up it asks "still want it?" — talk through the why; it judges whether you actually need it.

3. Decided against? Have it draft the cancellation message; if it already shipped, it teaches you the cheapest way to refuse delivery.

**Real talk:** Canceling and refusing delivery are hands-on, by you — it just helps you decide.

</details>

<a id="skill-258"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>258. Is That Deposit Deal Actually a Deal?</b> —— "Pay 50, get 500 off!" — can't tell if you're saving or being played…</summary>

"Pay 50, get 500 off" deposit promos — can't tell if you're saving or being played.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot the presale's deposit terms for Muse.

2. Tell it: "Do the math: deposit plus final payment — how much cheaper than buying outright?"

3. Don't forget the final payment — have it set a reminder. Miss it and the deposit's gone.

**Real talk:** Deposits and final payments are your taps — it does the math, and it can't save you from a missed deadline.

</details>

<a id="skill-259"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>259. E-Commerce Sale Calendar</b> —— Bought at full price — the big sale starts the next day. Want to eat the receipt.</summary>

Bought at full price — the big sale starts the next day. Makes you want to eat the receipt.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "Give me this year's sale calendar: when are 618, Double 11, and the New Year shopping festival?"

2. Eyeing something big? Ask first: "How many days till the next sale — how much would waiting save?"

3. A week before each sale, have it remind you — park your picks in the cart early.

**Real talk:** Its dates follow past patterns — the platform's official announcement is what counts.

</details>

<a id="skill-260"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>260. Is That Buy-One-Get-One Actually Worth It?</b> —— BOGO looks sweet — the free one's a mini you'll never use. Zero saved.</summary>

"Buy one get one" looks sweet — the free one's a mini size you'll never use. Zero saved.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot the promo terms for Muse.

2. Tell it: "Unit-price this: how much cheaper than usual, and would I even use the freebie?"

3. Worth it? Buy. Not worth it? Have it find the regular price of the same item for comparison.

**Real talk:** Unit pricing is on it; but "buy or not" is your call — it doesn't know how much shampoo you have left.

</details>
<a id="skill-261"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>261. Parcel Forwarder Showdown</b> —— A dozen forwarders — shipping differs 2x, pick wrong and get taxed.</summary>

A dozen parcel forwarders — shipping costs differ by 2x, pick wrong and you get taxed.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse what you're buying: weight, category, shipping country.

2. Tell it: "Compare forwarder routes: cost, speed, tax-hit odds."

3. Once picked, have it log the tracking number — it nudges you if customs stalls.

**Real talk:** Ordering and customs run through the forwarder, by you — it just compares and picks.

</details>

<a id="skill-262"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>262. When to (Re)Subscribe</b> —— Renewed at full price — the platform goes half-off the next day.</summary>

Renewed the membership at full price — the platform goes half-off the next day.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your regular memberships: video, music, cloud storage.

2. Tell it: "Watch these for promos — what's the historical low, when's the cheapest time to buy?"

3. Near expiry, it advises: wait for the sale or renew now — helps you decide.

**Real talk:** Subscribing is your tap — it just calls the timing. See a low price and dawdle? It can't grab it for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-263"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>263. Donate or Swap Your Idle Stuff</b> —— Listed secondhand for half a month, zero bites — just taking up space.</summary>

Listed secondhand for half a month, zero bites — just taking up space.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph the idle items for Muse.

2. Tell it: "Judge: worth selling secondhand, or donate/swap and be done?"

3. Donating? Have it find nearby drop-off points. Swapping? It drafts your swap post.

**Real talk:** Shipping and meetups are in person, by you — it advises and writes copy.

</details>

<a id="skill-264"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>264. Are Those Mega-Subsidy Deals Legit?</b> —— 300 cheaper than official — scared it's a refurb, never dared to buy.</summary>

300 yuan cheaper than the official store under the mega-subsidy deals — scared it's a refurb, never dared to buy.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the product link or screenshot.

2. Tell it: "Check this model's subsidy price history — real discount or markup-then-discount?"

3. If you buy, have it walk you through inspection: serial number, activation date.

**Real talk:** It checks prices and teaches inspection — authenticity calls belong to official service diagnostics.

</details>

<a id="skill-265"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>265. Grocery App Price Compare</b> —— Same bunch of greens — 1+ yuan apart across three apps. Daily buying adds up…</summary>

Same bunch of greens — over 1 yuan apart across three grocery apps. Buy daily and it adds up.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. List your staples for Muse: eggs, greens, pork.

2. Tell it: "Compare these apps today — who's cheapest?"

3. Have it run the comparison weekly — factoring in each app's coupons too.

**Real talk:** Ordering is your tap in the app — it just tells you who's cheapest.

</details>

<a id="skill-266"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>266. Stacking Food-Delivery Coupons</b> —— Stack of coupons claimed — at checkout, only one applies. Wasted claims.</summary>

Claimed a stack of delivery coupons — at checkout only one applies. Wasted claims.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot your coupons from each platform for Muse.

2. Tell it: "Teach me the stacking: coupons, order discounts, member perks — the cheapest combo."

3. Before ordering, ask: "This order's 45 yuan — what's the optimal play?" It gives you the best combo.

**Real talk:** Claiming and ordering happen in the app, by you — it just does the stacking math.

</details>

<a id="skill-267"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>267. Coffee Coupon Stockpiling</b> —— Paying full price daily — others stockpile at 9.9 a cup while you pay 30…</summary>

Paying full price for coffee daily — others stockpile coupons at 9.9 a cup while you pay 30.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "My usual brands — teach me to stockpile: where are the multi-packs, the bank discounts?"

2. Have it do the math on your consumption: how many to hoard without waste, how much saved.

3. Expiry approaching? It nudges you to drink up — don't let coupons die.

**Real talk:** Buying coupons is your tap in the mini-app — it just points you to the deals.

</details>

<a id="skill-268"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>268. Gas Station Discounts</b> —— 300 yuan a fill-up, never used a discount — pure overpay.</summary>

300 yuan a fill-up, never used a single discount — pure overpay.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "My usual routes — which nearby stations run the best promos?"

2. Have it teach the stacking: station app coupons, bank discounts, cheapest weekday.

3. Before each fill-up, ask: "Where's cheapest today?"

**Real talk:** Fueling and paying are in person, by you — it just knows the playbook. Price swings? Out of its control.

</details>

<a id="skill-269"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>269. Near-Expiry Food Bargains</b> —— Half-price and still fresh — but what if it's actually gone bad?</summary>

Near-expiry food at half price, still fresh — but what if it's actually gone bad?

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "Teach me the near-expiry rules: how many days left is buyable, which categories to never touch near-expiry."

2. In the store, photograph a deal and have it judge: "Worth it at this discount?"

3. After buying, have it sort by expiry — eat the soonest first.

**Real talk:** Rules and sorting are on it; but picking up, putting down, and checking dates in the store are you — an upset stomach isn't on its tab.

</details>

<a id="skill-270"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>270. Bulk-Buy Stockpiling</b> —— Bulk looks cheap — until it expires uneaten. Even pricier.</summary>

Bulk packs look cheap — until they expire uneaten. Even pricier.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse what you want to stock: tissues, detergent, rice and flour.

2. Tell it: "Unit-price the bulk pack — how much cheaper, and how long till my household finishes it?"

3. Only stock what you'll finish — it reminds you to restock on pace. Never run out, never hoard.

**Real talk:** It does the math; ordering and stockpiling are you — and it can't eat your leftovers.

</details>

<a id="skill-271"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>271. Secondhand Haggling Scripts</b> —— Eyeing a used camera — want to haggle but scared of offending the seller.</summary>

Eyeing a used camera on a resale platform — want to haggle but scared one wrong line torches the deal.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the listing link.

2. Tell it: "Check the going secondhand rate for this model — set my target price, and write me an inoffensive opener."

3. Seller counters? Screenshot the chat — it coaches your next line.

**Real talk:** The haggling chat is yours to send — it writes the scripts and sets the strategy.

</details>

<a id="skill-272"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>272. Don't Miss Payment Perks</b> —— UnionPay deals, bank rebates, daily — you pay full price every time.</summary>

UnionPay app deals, bank instant rebates — daily. You pay full price every time.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "My usual bank cards — build me a payment-perks cheat sheet."

2. Have it tell you each morning: "Which bank has instant rebates today, what purchases qualify?"

3. Before a big purchase, ask: "This one's 2,000 yuan — what's the cheapest way to pay?"

**Real talk:** Binding cards and claiming coupons happen in the banking apps, by you — it just knows the playbook.

</details>

<a id="skill-273"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>273. Free-Shipping Threshold Top-Up</b> —— 5 yuan short of free shipping — pad with a 20-yuan item. The padding costs more.</summary>

5 yuan short of free shipping — pad it with a 20-yuan item. The padding costs more than the shipping.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot your cart for Muse.

2. Tell it: "How short am I — find the cheapest top-up item. Don't make me overspend."

3. Pick consumables for the top-up: tissues, trash bags — you'll use them anyway.

**Real talk:** Ordering is your tap — it just does the cheapest-top-up math.

</details>

<a id="skill-274"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>274. Presale Deposit or In-Stock?</b> —— Presale's 50 cheaper but ships in half a month — in-stock arrives tomorrow. Torn.</summary>

Presale is 50 cheaper but ships in half a month — in-stock costs more but arrives tomorrow. Torn.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse both the presale and in-stock links.

2. Tell it: "Compare: price gap, wait time, gift difference — make the call for me."

3. Not urgent? Wait for presale. Urgent? Go in-stock — and have it set a price-drop alert so you can claim protection if in-stock drops.

**Real talk:** Comparing is on it; but presale-vs-stock and the payment tap are yours — the money's in your pocket, not its.

</details>

<a id="skill-275"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>275. Second-Item-Half-Off Splits</b> —— "Second item half off" — dragged a friend in, still waiting on their share three months later…</summary>

"Second item half off" — dragged a friend into the split, still waiting on their share three months later.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot the promo rules for Muse.

2. Tell it: "Do the math: fifty-fifty split — what does each of us actually pay, how much cheaper than usual?"

3. Before splitting, have it draft you a "transfer first, then I order" message. Clear accounts, even with friends.

**Real talk:** Collecting money and ordering are in person, by you — it does the math and the messaging.

</details>

<a id="skill-276"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>276. Bundle vs. Solo</b> —— The hair-care bundle looks like a deal — but you'd never touch the conditioner.</summary>

The hair-care bundle looks like a deal — but there's a conditioner in there you'd never touch.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse both the bundle and solo prices.

2. Tell it: "Unit-price it — for the items I'd actually use, does the bundle really save?"

3. Don't bundle-buy items you won't use — have it find the lowest solo price.

**Real talk:** Unit pricing is on it; but bundle-vs-solo is your call — it doesn't know how long your conditioner has lasted.

</details>

<a id="skill-277"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>277. Is the Extended Warranty a Scam?</b> —— Upsold 500 yuan of extended warranty — three years, zero breakdowns. Money torched.</summary>

Upsold 500 yuan of extended warranty on an appliance — three years, zero breakdowns. Money torched.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Give Muse the model and the warranty price.

2. Tell it: "Look up this model's failure rate and what the warranty covers — is the 500 worth it?"

3. Buy it for failure-prone stuff, skip it for tanks — have it make the call.

**Real talk:** It reads public reputation data — actual claims follow the policy terms.

</details>

<a id="skill-278"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>278. Luxury Samples Before You Commit</b> —— Thousand-yuan serum — turns out you're allergic. Money down the drain.</summary>

Thousand-yuan name-brand serum — turns out you're allergic. Money down the drain.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "I want to try this brand's serum — find me legit sample/trial-size channels."

2. Have it compare: how much pricier per ml are samples — is the trial cost worth it?

3. If the trial works, buy full-size — have it watch for the historical low.

**Real talk:** Ordering samples is your tap — it finds channels and does the math.

</details>

<a id="skill-279"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>279. Group-Buy Voucher Expiry Alerts</b> —— The buffet voucher expired before you remembered — 200 yuan down the drain.</summary>

The group-buy buffet voucher expired before you remembered — 200 yuan down the drain.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot your group-buy vouchers for Muse.

2. Tell it: "Sort by expiry — warn me 7 days before each one dies."

3. When the nudge comes, book the meal fast — or have it teach you to gift or refund it.

**Real talk:** Booking and refunding happen in the app, by you — it just reminds.

</details>

<a id="skill-280"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>280. Cheaper Movie Tickets</b> —— 45 a ticket in the app — everyone else paid 25.</summary>

45 yuan a ticket in the app — everyone else paid 25.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "I want to see this movie — which platform sells cheapest?"

2. Have it teach the stacking: platform coupons, bank rebates, member pricing.

3. Before checkout, ask once more: "Any cheaper channel?"

**Real talk:** Buying the ticket is your tap in the app — it just tells you who's cheapest.

</details>

<a id="skill-281"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>281. Cheaper Car Washes</b> —— 50 a wash, twice a month — nearly a thousand a year.</summary>

50 yuan a wash, twice a month — nearly a thousand a year.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "I live near this compound — which nearby car wash is the best value?"

2. Have it compare: single wash, monthly plan, group-buy vouchers — which wins?

3. Once picked, have it remind you: voucher expiring, plan running out — time to renew.

**Real talk:** You drive there yourself — it just finds the deals.

</details>

<a id="skill-282"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>282. Prepaid Top-Up Card Traps</b> —— Salon: top up 2,000, get 500 free — three months later the shop's gone.</summary>

Salon deal: top up 2,000, get 500 free — three months later the shop's gone.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph the shop's top-up plan for Muse before signing.

2. Tell it: "Check this shop's reputation — any vanishing history? Is this card worth it?"

3. Even if yes, don't load much — have it calculate the sweet spot: enough to use up, nothing wasted.

**Real talk:** It reads public info — it can't guarantee the shop won't vanish. Big top-ups are your judgment call.

</details>

<a id="skill-283"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>283. Is "Interest-Free" Installments Actually Free?</b> —— "Interest-free installments" — until the fees work out to 15% APR.</summary>

"Interest-free installments" — until the fees work out to 15% APR.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot the installment plan for Muse.

2. Tell it: "Do the math: per-period payment, any fees, what's the real APR?"

3. Only split if it's truly free and fee-less — otherwise have it compare against paying in full.

**Real talk:** It does the math; installments-vs-full and which button to tap are your decisions.

</details>

<a id="skill-284"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>284. Off-Season Clearance Scores</b> —— Down jacket half-price in summer — but will it look dated next year?</summary>

Down jacket half-price in summer — but will it look dated next year?

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "I want an off-season down jacket — teach me to pick timeless cuts, not trendy ones."

2. Have it compare: clearance price vs. regular — how much are you really saving?

3. Unsure on size? Have it check review sizing advice — don't buy the wrong size chasing cheap.

**Real talk:** Ordering and trying on are yours — it compares prices and picks styles.

</details>

<a id="skill-285"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>285. Personal-Shopper Pitfalls</b> —— Bag via an overseas personal shopper, 2,000 cheaper — turned out fake.</summary>

Bag through an overseas personal shopper, 2,000 cheaper — arrived a knockoff.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the shopper's quote and the item.

2. Tell it: "Check the boutique price — if the shopper's price is way under, is something off?"

3. Have it teach inspection: receipts, serial numbers, authenticity codes.

**Real talk:** It checks prices and teaches inspection — final authenticity calls belong to the brand's official verification.

</details>

<a id="skill-286"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>286. Baby Supply Stockpiling</b> —— Diapers and formula daily — full price costs thousands extra a year.</summary>

Diapers and formula, daily use — full price costs thousands extra a year.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your diaper and formula models.

2. Tell it: "Do the math on monthly burn — how much to stock without waste? Stock hardest at big sales."

3. Before each big sale, have it remind you — and compare platforms for the cheapest.

**Real talk:** Ordering and stockpiling are yours — it does the volume math and price compares.

</details>

<a id="skill-287"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>287. Drug Price Compare</b> —— Same ingredient — imported 80 a box, domestic 20. 4x apart…</summary>

Same active ingredient — imported 80 a box, domestic 20. 4x apart.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the prescribed drug name.

2. Tell it: "Find domestic equivalents with the same ingredient — price gap, are they legit?"

3. Before switching, have it remind you: ask your doctor first — don't swap on your own.

**Real talk:** It checks prices; what to take and whether to switch is the doctor's call — don't freelance it.

</details>

<a id="skill-288"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>288. Pet Supply Stockpiling</b> —— The furry overlord must be fed — full-price kibble burns treat money by the thousand.</summary>

The furry overlord must be fed — full-price kibble and chow burns a thousand-plus yuan a year that could've been treat money.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your usual kibble brands.

2. Tell it: "Do the math on monthly appetite — how much to stock at big sales without waste? Compare platforms."

3. Nudge me before each big sale — and check for stackable coupons.

**Real talk:** Ordering is your tap — it does the volume math and compares.</details>

---

## 📣 Content Creation & Growth

> Run accounts, write posts, ride trends

<a id="skill-289"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>289. De-AI Your Posts</b> —— Your posts scream AI — "firstly, secondly, in conclusion"…</summary>

Every post you write sounds AI-generated — "firstly, secondly, in conclusion" — and the views are miserable.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Drop your draft into Muse and say: "Keep my meaning, kill the AI smell."

2. Tell it: "Make it sound like I'm texting a friend — short sentences, and open with a line that stops the scroll."

3. Once the tone feels right, just post it. It'll flag the parts that still smell like AI.

**Real talk:** You still have to write the draft yourself — the ideas have to be yours. It just strips the AI flavor; you hit publish.

</details>

<a id="skill-290"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>290. Killer Comment Ideas</b> —— Tens of thousands of comments, and yours always sinks…</summary>

Tens of thousands of comments under a trending post — yours always sinks to the bottom while someone else's witty comment gets all the likes.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse the trending topic in one sentence: "Give me 5 killer comment ideas."

2. Tell it: "Short, sharp, impossible not to like. Hook them in the first line."

3. Pick the best one, rewrite it in your voice, and post. It even comes up with original jokes for you.

**Real talk:** You have to spot the trend yourself. It writes the jokes; you rewrite in your voice and hit send.

</details>

<a id="skill-291"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>291. Thread Outline Builder</b> —— Plenty to say, but your thread reads like a diary…</summary>

You want to write a long thread and have plenty to say, but it comes out like a rambling diary — nobody makes it past post three.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your topic in one sentence: "Give me an 8-post thread outline."

2. Tell it: "Post one must be a hook, one point per post in the middle, and end with a question for engagement."

3. Fill in each post from the outline, then have it check whether the pacing breaks before you post.

**Real talk:** The topic and the actual writing are on you — it doesn't have your material. It builds the skeleton; you add the meat.

</details>

<a id="skill-292"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>292. Image Prompts for Your Posts</b> —— Great post, ugly image, zero clicks — and you can't draw…</summary>

No matter how good the post is, an ugly image kills the clicks. You can't draw, and hunting for stock images eats your whole night.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your post: "Write me 3 image prompts that actually look good."

2. Pick one and have it generate the image. Not happy? "Try a different style."

3. Save the image, attach it when you post, and watch the views climb.

**Real talk:** You have to supply the post. It handles prompts and images, but pairing them and reading the numbers is on you.

</details>

<a id="skill-293"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>293. Headline Options for A/B Testing</b> —— Half an hour on the headline, still meh — post flops anyway…</summary>

You spend half an hour on the headline and still hate it. The post flops, and the post-mortem blames the headline.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the post body: "Give me 5 headline options, each in a different style."

2. Have it label each one's hook type: curiosity, conflict, benefit, counterintuitive.

3. Pick the two boldest — post one on X, one on WeChat Moments, and see which wins.

**Real talk:** You write the body. It writes the headlines; you pick, post, and read the numbers.

</details>

<a id="skill-294"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>294. Follower Growth Review</b> —— A month of posting, 30 new followers, no idea what worked…</summary>

A month of posting and you've gained 30 followers. No idea which posts worked — it's all vibes.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot your backend stats — views, likes, reposts, new followers — and send them to Muse.

2. Tell it: "Analyze which content performed best. Tell me what to post more and less of next week."

3. Plan next week's topics from its conclusions, then screenshot and review again. Closed loop.

**Real talk:** X doesn't give Muse API access, so you screenshot the backend yourself. It gives the conclusions; you execute.

</details>

<a id="skill-295"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>295. Topic Bank</b> —— Ideas strike on the subway and die in your notes app…</summary>

Inspiration hits on the subway and is gone by the time you're home. Good topics go to die in your notes app.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. The moment an idea hits, send Muse a one-liner: "Add this to my topic bank."

2. Each week tell it: "Look at the bank, pick 3 I can write this week, rank them."

3. After you write one, have it cross it off. The bank stays alive.

**Real talk:** The ideas have to come from you — it can't invent your thoughts. It stores and picks; you write.

</details>

<a id="skill-296"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>296. Cross-Platform Rewrites</b> —— One great post, one platform — the rest wasted…</summary>

You write one great post and publish it on a single platform. The others sit empty, and all that traffic goes to waste.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the original: "Rewrite this for Xiaohongshu (China's lifestyle content app), X, and my WeChat Official Account."

2. Tell it: "Xiaohongshu needs emojis and short paragraphs, X needs a short thread, the Official Account needs a deep long-form."

3. Save all three versions, publish one a day — one post, three helpings of traffic.

**Real talk:** You write the original. It handles the rewrites, but you publish on all three platforms yourself — it can't log into your accounts.

</details>

<a id="skill-297"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>297. Posting Cadence Nudges</b> —— You want to post daily, quit after three days, then vanish…</summary>

You want to post daily, but you quit after three days. Fans just started remembering you, and you're gone again.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Set a goal with Muse: "I want to post 4 times a week — hold me to it."

2. Have it ask you at a fixed time daily: "Did you post today?" Miss a day and it nags you.

3. After a 30-day streak, have it write you a consistency report — post it and gain followers.

**Real talk:** It does all the tracking and nagging. But you have to write the posts — it can nag a hundred times, and if you don't write, nothing goes up.

</details>

<a id="skill-298"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>298. Viral Post Breakdowns</b> —— Their post: 100K likes. Yours: 10. What are you missing?…</summary>

Someone else's post gets 100K likes; yours gets 10. You want to learn, but can't figure out what to copy.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Copy a viral post's full text to Muse: "Break down why this blew up."

2. Tell it: "Split it into hook, structure, emotion, engagement — one sentence each."

3. Glance at the breakdown before your next post and fill its skeleton with your own material.

**Real talk:** You find the viral post. It breaks down the skeleton; you supply the meat and write the hit.

</details>

<a id="skill-299"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>299. Xiaohongshu Viral Title Formulas</b> —— Good post, manual-style headline — clicks are miserable…</summary>

The post is solid, but the headline reads like an instruction manual — fewer people click in than there are creators.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your finished post: "Using the Xiaohongshu viral title formula, give me 10 titles: number + pain point + result."

2. Have it sort the 10 into "curiosity," "how-to," and "emotional" buckets so you can pick a direction at a glance.

3. After publishing, send back the click data: "Whichever bucket wins, lean that way next time."

**Real talk:** It can't read the recommendation algorithm — check the click data in your backend yourself. It just supplies options.

</details>

<a id="skill-300"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>300. 3-Second Video Hooks</b> —— Flat first three seconds — viewers swipe faster than you edit.</summary>

Your video's first three seconds are flat narration. Viewers swipe away faster than you can edit.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your video topic in one sentence: "Write 5 three-second opening hooks — conflict, suspense, no fluff."

2. Have it pair each hook with a visual: "What the first line says, what the shot shows — line by line."

3. After filming, send it the first three seconds: "Is this hook sharp enough, or will viewers swipe?"

**Real talk:** It can't judge your finished footage's feel — watch the final cut yourself to decide if the hook and pacing land.

</details>

<a id="skill-301"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>301. Thumbnail Text That Pops in 3 Seconds</b> —— Tiny crowded thumbnail text — scrolled past in 3 seconds.</summary>

Your thumbnail text is tiny and crowded. People won't even give it three seconds — straight past.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your video topic: "Thumbnail gets 8 characters max. Give me 5 versions, big and legible."

2. Have it sort by "pain-point," "result," or "question" type — you pick the loudest one.

3. Screenshot the finished thumbnail and send it back: "Is the text big enough to read on a phone?"

**Real talk:** It supplies all 5 versions, but you pick the loudest and make the graphic — taste calls are yours, not its.

</details>

<a id="skill-302"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>302. Livestream Promo Copy</b> —— "Going live at 8 tonight" — and three people show up…</summary>

You toss out a "going live at 8 tonight" before the stream, and three people show up.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your stream topic and perks: "Write a promo: pain point + perk + time, under 100 characters."

2. Have it do two versions — "one for Moments, one for the fan group, different vibes."

3. Two hours before go-live, have it write a reminder: "Last 2 hours — miss it and wait a week."

**Real talk:** It writes the promos, but you schedule and post them yourself — it can't log into platforms for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-303"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>303. Fan Group Welcome Message</b> —— New members get ignored, lurk three days, then leave…</summary>

New members join the group and nobody greets them. They lurk for three days, then leave. The group keeps going colder.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your account's positioning: "Write a new-member welcome: intro + rules + one icebreaker."

2. Have it add an engagement hook: "New here? Drop your city and the problem you want solved, and claim a welcome gift."

3. Have it refresh the version weekly: "Whatever this week's theme is, the welcome rides it."

**Real talk:** It writes the welcome, but you paste it into the group and actually chat with newcomers — it can't get into your WeChat group.

</details>

<a id="skill-304"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>304. Trend-Tracking Calendar</b> —— You hear about trends three days late — the soup's cold…</summary>

You find out about a trend three days after it breaks. By the time you chase it, the soup's cold.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "List the holidays, award shows, and launches worth riding for the next 30 days — as a calendar."

2. Have it pair each trend with one line: "How does this connect to my niche?"

3. Ask it every morning: "What's trending today, and what angle should I take?"

**Real talk:** It only sees public info — on breaking trends it may be slower than you. Keep a few of your own sources if you really chase trends.

</details>

<a id="skill-305"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>305. Plagiarism Self-Check</b> —— Borrowed from viral posts — "inspired by" or just copied?…</summary>

You referenced a few viral posts, and now you feel guilty: is this borrowing or copying?

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your draft plus the reference posts: "Compare section by section — flag overlapping structure, examples, and punchlines."

2. Have it score the overlap: "More than 3 matches gets flagged in red, with rewrite advice."

3. Rewrite per its advice, then check again: "Does this still look copied?"

**Real talk:** It does all the comparison, but the rewriting — in your own words — is on you. It can't write for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-306"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>306. Article Layout Check</b> —— Great article, 8-line paragraphs on mobile — readers bounce.</summary>

The article is good, but on a phone it's 8 lines per paragraph. Readers just close it.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your draft: "Check for mobile reading — max 3 lines per paragraph, highlight the key sentences."

2. Have it add subheads: "One every 300 characters, so skimmers still get the point."

3. Before finalizing, have it read through: "Any typos, awkward lines, off tone?"

**Real talk:** It gives layout advice, but preview it on your own phone before publishing — it can't see the real rendering.

</details>

<a id="skill-307"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>307. Podcast Episode Show Notes</b> —— Episode recorded, nobody writes the description…</summary>

The podcast episode is recorded but nobody writes the description. Listeners click in with no idea what it's about.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the episode outline or transcript: "Write show notes — 3-sentence intro plus timeline plus books and links mentioned."

2. Have it pull 3 quotable lines — "the kind you can screenshot and post to Moments."

3. Before the next recording, ask it: "What follow-up would last episode's listeners want most?"

**Real talk:** Show notes and quotable lines are all its job. But recording, editing, and publishing are all you — it can't open its mouth for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-308"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>308. Short-Video Shot List</b> —— Ideas in your head, chaos on camera — key shots missing…</summary>

You have the idea in your head, but the moment the camera's on it's chaos — and you discover you missed key shots.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the topic: "Write the script as a shot list — shot number, framing, lines, duration. 60 seconds total."

2. Have it mark the transitions: "Cut here, add a sound effect there — no single-take marathons."

3. After filming, check against the list: "Which shots did I miss? Make me a reshoot list."

**Real talk:** Shot lists and transitions are its job. But holding the phone, filming, and reshooting missed shots — that's you. It can't press record.

</details>

<a id="skill-309"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>309. Replies for Troll Comments</b> —— Troll in the comments — reply looks cheap, silence stings…</summary>

Someone's picking a fight in your comments. Reply and you look cheap; don't reply and it eats at you.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot the comment for Muse: "Write 3 replies — one funny deflection, one firm clapback, one graceful ignore."

2. Have it match your account's persona: "Whatever my usual style is, the reply matches it."

3. Pick one and post it. "Same template next time this happens."

**Real talk:** It only drafts replies — you hit send and block. It can't log into your account.

</details>

<a id="skill-310"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>310. Fan Submission Calls</b> —— You call for submissions — and hear crickets…</summary>

You want fan submissions, but the call goes out and the inbox stays empty.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse the theme: "Write a call for submissions — what to submit, how, and what's in it for them."

2. Have it add a barrier-lowering line: "Phone snapshots are fine, no pro gear needed."

3. Once submissions arrive, have it sort them: "Group by theme, pick the 10 best."

**Real talk:** The call and the sorting are its job. But which 10 are the standouts — that's your call. Taste is yours, not its.

</details>

<a id="skill-311"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>311. Giveaway Copy</b> —— Your giveaway reads like a memo — nobody shares it…</summary>

Your giveaway copy reads like an office memo. Fewer people share it than you have friends.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse the prize and rules: "Write giveaway copy — how sweet the prize is, how easy it is to enter. 50 characters."

2. Have it add urgency: "Drawing Friday 8pm — miss it and kick yourself."

3. After the drawing, have it write the announcement: "Winners list plus thanks plus next-time teaser."

**Real talk:** It can't run the giveaway for you — you operate the platform's giveaway tool yourself. Copy is its department.

</details>

<a id="skill-312"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>312. Pinned Post Intro</b> —— Pinned post is ancient — new fans don't know who you are…</summary>

New fans visit your profile and the pinned post is from six months ago. They have no idea what you do.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your background and greatest hits: "Write a pinned intro — who I am, what I post, what following me gets you."

2. Keep it under 100 characters: "Readable in 3 seconds, nothing missing."

3. Update it quarterly: "Whatever I achieved this quarter goes in."

**Real talk:** The 100-character intro is its job. But publishing and updating it quarterly — that's you. It can't log into your account.

</details>

<a id="skill-313"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>313. Content Series Planning</b> —— Series dies at episode 3 — ideas gone, story unfinished…</summary>

You want to do a series, but by episode three the ideas dry up. Abandoned.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse the series theme: "Plan 10 episodes — one-line title plus the hook for each."

2. Have it order them: "Hooks up front, meaty stuff in the middle, a wrap-up at the end."

3. After each episode, ask it: "How does the next one pick up from this one? Give me the bridge line."

**Real talk:** Planning 10 episodes is its job. But writing them one by one — that's you. It can't post for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-314"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>314. Comeback Announcement</b> —— Gone two months, back posting — fans go "who are you?"…</summary>

You vanish for two months, then come back posting like nothing happened. Fans are confused: who are you?

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse why you were gone in one line: "Write a comeback post — sincere, no sob story, say what's coming next."

2. Have it add a perk for old fans: "First week back, one extra post a day."

3. After posting, have it draft a comment reply template: "For everyone asking where I went."

**Real talk:** The announcement and the perks are its job. But hitting post and facing your old fans — that's you. Sincerity can't be outsourced.

</details>

<a id="skill-315"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>315. Sponsorship Rate Card</b> —— Brand asks your rate, you blurt a number — instant regret…</summary>

A brand asks for your rates and you blurt out a number off the top of your head. Regret sets in immediately — too low.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your follower count, average views, and conversion cases: "Set me three pricing tiers at market rate."

2. Have it write a formal rate card: "Line items — what price, what deliverables, how many revisions."

3. When they haggle, ask it: "Can I take this price? Give me my walk-away lines."

**Real talk:** It only gives reference numbers. Negotiating, signing, and collecting — all you. It can't talk money for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-316"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>316. Natural Ad Integration</b> —— The ad lands like a brick — comments scream "sellout"…</summary>

You take a sponsorship and the ad lands like a brick. The comments are all calling you a sellout.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the product's selling points and your topic: "Give me 3 integration angles — the ad should feel like part of the content."

2. Have it write the transition: "Slide from content to product naturally, two sentences max."

3. When it's written, have it check: "How ad-heavy does this feel? At which second would readers smell the ad?"

**Real talk:** Angles and transitions are its job. But negotiating and signing with the brand — that's you. It can't talk money.

</details>

<a id="skill-317"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>317. Livestream Outline</b> —— No outline, you ramble — zero viewer questions answered…</summary>

You go live without an outline, ramble off-topic, and answer none of the viewers' questions.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your stream topic: "Outline 60 minutes — 5 min opener, 40 min substance, 15 min Q&A."

2. Have it add a hook per segment: "Drop a question here so viewers don't swipe away."

3. Keep the outline beside you during the stream: "Wherever you are, you can pick right back up."

**Real talk:** Outlines and hooks are its job. But talking to a camera for 60 minutes — that's you. It can't show its face for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-318"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>318. Fan Comment Reply Scripts</b> —— Fan asks for a tutorial, you reply "ok" — they're gone…</summary>

A fan comments asking for a tutorial, and you reply "ok." They never come back.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the 10 comment types you get most: "Write 3 replies each — sincere, specific, with a follow-up question."

2. Have it add a nudge: "After replying, ask one more question to keep the chat going."

3. Refresh weekly: "Fold this week's memes in so the scripts don't go stale."

**Real talk:** It writes the scripts, but you send them one by one — it can't log into your account.

</details>

<a id="skill-319"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>319. Competitor Account Breakdown</b> —— Study peers without becoming a bad copy…</summary>

You want to learn from competitors, but you're afraid of becoming a pale imitation — and end up a mess of both.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot a competitor's profile for Muse: "Break down their positioning, topics, posting cadence, and monetization."

2. Have it list 3 things worth learning: "Steal the method, not the content."

3. Have it list 3 pitfalls to avoid: "Where they crashed — you steer around it."

**Real talk:** Breakdowns and pitfall lists are its job. But which three to learn and which pit to dodge — that judgment is yours. You walk your own road.

</details>

<a id="skill-320"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>320. Content Calendar</b> —— Post when inspired, vanish when not — looks manic…</summary>

You post when inspiration strikes and go dark when it doesn't. Your account looks manic.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your topic pool: "Build a 30-day content calendar — what goes up when, in what format."

2. Have it mix the rhythm: "Meaty posts to light ones at 3 to 1 — don't exhaust your followers."

3. Every Sunday, have it review: "Which post did well this week? Schedule more like it."

**Real talk:** It can build the schedule, but you set the timers and publish — it can't log into platforms for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-321"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>321. Clickbait Guardrails</b> —— Headline oversells — unfollows outpace follows…</summary>

You get carried away with the headline. People click in, cry clickbait, and unfollows outpace follows.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the headline plus the body: "Check whether the headline overpromises — can the body deliver?"

2. Have it score the headline: "Out of 10. Anything under 7 gets a more honest rewrite."

3. After publishing, watch the comments: "If anyone cries clickbait, send me their exact words — avoid it next time."

**Real talk:** Checking and scoring are its job. But which headline ships — that's your call. And if it flops, you're the one getting yelled at.

</details>

<a id="skill-322"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>322. Long-Video Chapters</b> —— 20 minutes, one take — viewers bail halfway…</summary>

A 20-minute video in one continuous take. Viewers bail halfway through.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your video outline: "Split into 5 chapters — one mini-title plus one selling point each."

2. Have it write the transitions: "End each chapter so they can't resist the next."

3. Post the chapter timestamps: "Viewers can jump around — that's how completion rates climb."

**Real talk:** Chapters and transitions are its job. But editing, timestamps, and publishing — all you. It can't get into your editing software.

</details>

<a id="skill-323"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>323. Livestream Clip Repurposing</b> —— Two hours of value — unclipped, like it never happened…</summary>

Two hours of livestream, all substance. Without clips, it's like you never went live.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the stream transcript: "Find 5 highlight moments — quotable line plus timestamp, 30 seconds each side, each standing alone."

2. Have it title each clip: "A little clickbaity — clips don't get clicks without titles."

3. After editing per its list: "Each clip gets one line driving viewers back to the replay."

**Real talk:** It can't edit for you. It marks the timestamps; the cutting and publishing are yours.

</details>

<a id="skill-324"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>324. Fan Group Topic Warm-Ups</b> —— "Good morning" and group buys — the group is dead…</summary>

The group chat gets nothing but "good morning" and group-buy links. It's active like a dead group.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse what you're posting this week: "Design 3 group topics, warm them up in the chat three days early."

2. Have it write the opener: "One question plus one poll — fewer words, better."

3. Once the topic takes off: "Collect the group's best lines — that's next episode's material."

**Real talk:** Topics and openers are its job. But posting in the group and chatting with fans — that's you. It can't do your socializing.

</details>

<a id="skill-325"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>325. Newsletter Topics</b> —— Three issues in, out of topics — open rates sliding…</summary>

Three newsletter issues in and you're out of topics. Open rates slide lower every issue.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your niche and reader profile: "List 10 topics — 5 meaty, 3 opinion, 2 light."

2. Have it write a one-line teaser per topic: "The teaser decides whether they open it."

3. After sending, check the open rate: "Whatever topic type flops gets cut next issue."

**Real talk:** Topics and teasers are its job. But writing the actual issue and hitting send — that's you. Readers subscribed to you, not it.

</details>

<a id="skill-326"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>326. Talking-Head Scripts</b> —— Freeze on camera — a dozen takes for 30 seconds…</summary>

You stutter the moment the camera's on. A dozen botched takes — one 30-second clip takes all afternoon.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse what you need to say: "Turn it into a talking-head script — conversational, short sentences, one idea per line."

2. Have it mark emphasis and pauses: "Stress here, pause there — that's what makes delivery sound natural."

3. Watch the playback: "Wherever you stumbled, send it back and have it smooth that line out."

**Real talk:** Scripts and emphasis marks are its job. But recording take after take until it flows — that's you. It can't open your mouth for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-327"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>327. Pinned Comment Funnel</b> —— Buzzing comments, empty pinned slot — ad space wasted…</summary>

The comments are buzzing, but the pinned slot sits empty. A free ad slot, wasted.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse the video's topic: "Write a pinned comment — drive follows plus tease the next episode, one sentence."

2. Have it add a hook: "Want the full version? Check my profile."

3. New topic, new pinned comment — "Never repeat one across videos."

**Real talk:** It only writes the comment — you do the pinning yourself. It can't get into your account.

</details>

<a id="skill-328"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>328. Profile Bio Tune-Up</b> —— "I'm so-and-so" — nobody knows why to follow…</summary>

Your bio just says "I'm so-and-so." Three seconds later, new fans still don't know what following you gets them.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your positioning and greatest hits: "Rewrite the bio — who you are plus what you post plus what following gets you. Three lines."

2. Have it do two versions: "One professional, one warm — you pick."

3. A week later, check follower growth: "No change? Swap in the other version."

**Real talk:** Both versions are its job. But pasting it into your profile and swapping by the numbers — that's you. The account is in your hands.

</details>

<a id="skill-329"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>329. Hijacking Trending Comment Sections</b> —— Your comment drowns in the crowd — zero likes…</summary>

Tens of thousands of comments under a trending post, and yours drowns in the crowd. Zero likes.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the trending topic: "Write 5 killer comments — sharp angle, under 15 characters, with a meme."

2. Have it tune the voice per platform: "Weibo (China's microblogging platform) wants speed, Xiaohongshu wants detail, Douyin (China's short-video app) wants memes."

3. After posting, watch for an hour: "Whichever style gets likes — that's your formula going forward."

**Real talk:** It only writes the comments — posting fast and grabbing the top spot takes your own quick fingers. It can't post for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-330"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>330. Brand Pitch Letters</b> —— DMs to brands vanish — not even a read receipt…</summary>

You want sponsorships, but your DMs to brands vanish into the void — not even a read receipt.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot your stats for Muse: "Write a pitch — numbers plus cases plus how we'd work together, under 200 characters."

2. Have it add a hook: "I reviewed a similar product once — my conversion rate was 2x the average."

3. When they reply: "Send me the chat log — it drafts your negotiation lines."

**Real talk:** It can't DM for you — brand outreach, pricing, and contracts are all in person. That's you.

</details>

<a id="skill-331"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>331. Series Finale Post</b> —— Series ends, you go quiet — loyal fans are lost…</summary>

The series wraps and you just go quiet. Fans who followed along are lost.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse all the series titles: "Write a finale — highlight reel plus thanks plus a teaser for the next series."

2. Have it build a collection index: "Episode 1 covered X, episode 2 covered Y — scannable at a glance."

3. Pin it for a week after posting: "New fans see this first — it's the whole series in one."

**Real talk:** The finale and the index are its job. But publishing and pinning for a week — that's you. It can't log into your account.

</details>

<a id="skill-332"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>332. Topic Polls</b> —— Guessing what to film next — nobody watches…</summary>

You can't decide what to film next, so you guess — and nobody watches.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse 3 candidate topics: "Write one poll line each — sell the hook, don't spoil it."

2. Have it design the format: "Comment 1, 2, or 3, or run a real poll — depends on your platform."

3. When voting ends: "The winner gets a full outline, straight from it."

**Real talk:** Copy and format are its job. But launching the poll and counting votes — that's you. It can't see your comments.

</details>

---

## ✈️ Travel & Booking

> Flights, hotels, itineraries, visa paperwork

<a id="skill-333"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>333. Flight Delay Watchdog</b> —— Two hours early — then a 3-hour delay in the terminal…</summary>

You get to the airport two hours early, then the flight delays three hours. You're stuck in the terminal scrolling your phone.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your flight number and date: "Check the flight status 3 hours before takeoff — sync me on any change."

2. Add: "Tell me immediately if it's delayed over an hour, and pull up same-day rebooking options."

3. After landing, tell it "watch over" — it turns off the alerts itself, no babysitting needed.

**Real talk:** You report the flight number yourself. The watching is all its job — but rebooking and refunds happen in the airline app, by you. It can't do that for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-334"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>334. Flight Price Drop Alerts</b> —— Price drops ¥300 right after you buy — non-refundable…</summary>

The ticket drops 300 yuan right after you buy. It stings like swallowing a fly — and you can't refund it.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your origin, destination, and dates — have it check current prices first.

2. Tell it: "Alert me if the price drops more than 200 yuan — I'll decide when to pull the trigger."

3. After buying, ping it again — have it check whether the return leg has a similarly cheap date combo.

**Real talk:** Price checks and watching are its job. But step 3 — paying — is your tap. And if you snag a cheap fare, thank it.

</details>

<a id="skill-335"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>335. Hotel Price Comparison in One Go</b> —— Five apps open comparing — eyes glazed, still unsure…</summary>

Booking a hotel means four or five apps open comparing prices. Your eyes glaze over after half a day — and you're still scared of overpaying.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your target area, dates, and budget.

2. Have it check the same hotel across platforms: "List the all-in price with taxes, cheapest first."

3. Once you've decided, just say "this one" — have it walk you through booking to lock the room.

**Real talk:** Comparison is its job. But step 3's "this one" has to come from your mouth — and payment happens in the app, by you. It can't lock the room.

</details>

<a id="skill-336"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>336. Booking Emails → Itinerary Page</b> —— Confirmations scattered — pre-trip digging is hell…</summary>

Flight and hotel confirmations are scattered across your inbox. Digging them out before departure is a nightmare.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Forward your booking confirmations to Muse — or let it search your Gmail itself.

2. Tell it: "Turn these bookings into one itinerary, in chronological order."

3. Have it generate a mobile-friendly web version — and auto-send it to you the day before departure.

**Real talk:** You forward the confirmations yourself. Organizing and generating are its job — but you booked the flights and hotels, and it can't check you in.

</details>

<a id="skill-337"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>337. Visa Document Checklist</b> —— One missing document = another trip, hours wasted…</summary>

Miss one visa document and you're making another trip. Hours in line wasted, itinerary wrecked.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse which country and what visa type.

2. Tell it: "List the documents per official requirements — mark which need originals, which need translations."

3. Check them off one by one as you go — ask it where to get the missing ones and how long they take.

**Real talk:** You say where and what visa yourself. The checklist is its job — but you prepare the documents by hand, and you show up for the interview. It can't get the visa for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-338"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>338. Rent vs. Ride Cost Math</b> —— Three-day trip — rent or ride? Pure gut feel…</summary>

Three days out — is renting or ride-hailing cheaper? You're deciding by gut feel.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your rough route and number of days.

2. Have it estimate both totals: "Include gas, parking, and tolls."

3. Decide from the comparison — then have it pull real quotes from rental platforms.

**Real talk:** You report the route and days yourself. The math is its job — but picking up the rental, driving, and hitting the road are all you. It can't drive for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-339"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>339. Flight Price Comparison</b> —— Same flight, three prices — you overpaid by 300…</summary>

Same flight, three platforms, three prices. After buying, you realize you overpaid by 300.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your origin, destination, and dates: "Build a comparison — official site, OTAs, combo bookings. Who's cheapest?"

2. Have it teach you combo tricks: "Buy outbound here, return there — sometimes half the price."

3. Before paying, ask it: "Is this actually a good price? What's the historical range?"

**Real talk:** It can only compare — the actual paying tap is yours. It can't buy tickets.

</details>

<a id="skill-340"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>340. Hotel Red-Flag Checklist</b> —— 5-star photos, hostel reality — non-refundable…</summary>

The photos look five-star; the room feels like a hostel. And it's non-refundable.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot the hotel for Muse: "Scan the bad reviews for keywords — noise, hygiene, location. Flag the landmines."

2. Have it list 5 must-ask questions for the front desk: "Ask before check-in, not after the shock."

3. Have it find 2 same-price backups: "If this one bombs, switch immediately."

**Real talk:** Review-scanning and question lists are its job. But calling the front desk and inspecting the room — that's you. You're the one sleeping there, not it.

</details>

<a id="skill-341"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>341. Trip Itinerary Planning</b> —— Research on arrival — the day dies in lines and transit…</summary>

You only look up guides after arriving. The whole day dies in queues and transit.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your destination, days, and travel buddies: "Plan each day — morning, afternoon, evening."

2. Have it order stops by the map: "No east-side mornings and west-side afternoons — your legs will die."
