3. Leave half a day empty daily: "Plans change. Blank space is what makes it a vacation."

**Real talk:** Planning is its job. But hauling the suitcase out and walking 20,000 steps — that's you. It can't see the sights for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-342"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>342. Local Transit Guide</b> —— Foreign subway, charades at the ticket machine…</summary>

You're abroad and can't figure out the subway. Buying tickets is pure charades, and you're sweating.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your destination: "Sort out local transit — how to buy subway tickets, which ride app to use, are buses pricey?"

2. Have it make a translation cheat sheet: "What we call it at home vs. what they call it — don't board the wrong one."

3. Save it to your phone before departure: "Readable offline too."

**Real talk:** The guide and cheat sheet are its job. But buying the ticket, boarding, not riding the wrong direction — that's you. It can't squeeze into the subway for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-343"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>343. Refund/Change Policy Decoder</b> —— Three pages of fine print — still no idea of the cost…</summary>

Three pages of refund/change fine print, and you still don't know what you'll lose.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot your booking's refund terms for Muse: "Translate to human — how much do I lose refunding now vs. changing?"

2. Have it do the math: "Cheaper to refund and rebuy, or to change?"

3. Have it draft a line for customer service: "Say it like this for the best shot at a free change."

**Real talk:** The actual refund/change tap happens in the app, by you. It does the math and the scripts — not the confirming.

</details>

<a id="skill-344"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>344. Carpool Organizing</b> —— Solo rides cost too much — strangers might be weird…</summary>

Riding solo costs too much, but carpooling risks a nightmare seatmate.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your trip: "Write a carpool post — time, route, cost split, requirements. All clear."

2. Have it list 5 screening questions: "Ask before departure — filter the weirdos early."

3. Have it make a cost sheet: "How gas and tolls split — get it in writing first."

**Real talk:** Finding people, setting times, collecting money — all you. It writes the post and does the math.

</details>

<a id="skill-345"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>345. Camping Gear Checklist</b> —— Car full of gear — forgot the sleeping pad…</summary>

First camping trip: you bring a carload of stuff and forget the one thing that matters — the sleeping pad.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your headcount, days, and location: "Gear list in three buckets — must-bring, nice-to-have, leave behind."

2. Have it flag the 5 things beginners forget most: "Buy these before you're on the mountain remembering."

3. Update the list after the trip: "What went unused? Don't bring it next time."

**Real talk:** The list is its job. But buying the gear, hauling it up the mountain, pitching the tent — all you. It can't freeze for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-346"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>346. Traveling with Elderly Parents</b> —— Too packed and they're wiped; too loose and you lose…</summary>

Traveling with your parents: pack the schedule and they're exhausted; keep it loose and you feel shortchanged.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse their age and health: "Plan a slow itinerary — max 2 sights a day, 2-hour lunch breaks."

2. Have it check accessibility: "Elevators, wheelchairs, restrooms — these matter more than sights."

3. Have it list a med kit: "Daily meds plus emergency ones — none missing."

**Real talk:** It plans the itinerary, but you judge their health yourself — it can't make medical calls for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-347"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>347. Traveling with Kids</b> —— Traveling with a kid is war — one cry ruins everything…</summary>

Traveling with a kid is like going to war. One crying fit and the whole family's mood is gone.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse the kid's age: "Kid-friendly itinerary — sights plus playground plus nap. All three, no skipping."

2. Have it list the go-bag: "Snacks, spare clothes, sippy cup — the tantrum trio."

3. Have it prep 5 queue games: "No boring lines, no meltdowns."

**Real talk:** Itineraries and lists are its job. But carrying the kid, soothing the cries, pulling snacks out in line — that's you. It can't soothe your kid.

</details>

<a id="skill-348"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>348. Jet Lag Reset</b> —— 12-hour flight, days still flipped on day three…</summary>

A 12-hour flight, and three days later your days and nights are still flipped. Can't enjoy anything.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your flight times: "Build a jet-lag plan — shift bedtime 1 hour later daily for 3 days before departure."

2. Have it list an in-flight schedule: "When to sleep, when to eat — follow it."

3. After landing, ask it: "Drowsy in daylight — sunlight or coffee?"

**Real talk:** Plans and schedules are its job. But sleeping and waking on cue, getting out into the sun after landing — that's you. The jet lag lives in your body.

</details>

<a id="skill-349"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>349. Trip Expense Tracking</b> —— Back home — no idea where the money went…</summary>

Back from the trip with zero idea where the money went. Hurts and confusing.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Before departure, tell Muse: "Set up a trip ledger — transport, lodging, food, tickets. Four buckets."

2. Screenshot receipts daily and send them: "Auto-sorted, no math on your end."

3. After you're back, have it write the report: "Which bucket blew the budget? How should next time's budget look?"

**Real talk:** Sorting and reports are its job. But screenshotting every expense — that's you. If you don't log it, it can't magic up a ledger.

</details>

<a id="skill-350"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>350. Travel Emergency Kit</b> —— Lost passport abroad — brain goes blank…</summary>

You lose your passport abroad and your brain goes blank. No idea what to do first.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Before departure, have Muse list an emergency sheet: "Passport copies, insurance policy, embassy number — save to your phone."

2. Have it write the emergency flow: "Lost passport — step one, step two. Just follow it."

3. If it actually happens, ask it: "Given this situation, who do I contact first?"

**Real talk:** It only advises on emergencies. If you actually lose something and need the police, you make the call — it can't.

</details>

<a id="skill-351"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>351. Road Trip Route Planning</b> —— GPS tunnel vision — missed views, nearly missed gas…</summary>

You drive purely by GPS, miss all the scenery, and nearly run out of gas.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your start and end: "Plan the route — daily drive time, where to stop, which stretch has the best views."

2. Have it mark gas stations and rest areas: "Don't let the tank run dry — no stations in the mountains."

3. Have it check restrictions and roadwork: "Which stretch is under repair, which is restricted — detour early."

**Real talk:** It plans the route, but driving, fueling, and toll booths are all you. No substitute.

</details>

<a id="skill-352"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>352. High-Speed Rail Transfers</b> —— 20-minute transfer, sprinted anyway — missed it…</summary>

You leave 20 minutes for the transfer, sprint anyway, and still miss it. Ticket wasted.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse both train numbers: "Work out the transfer time — exit, security, re-entry. What's the minimum?"

2. Have it check the station layout: "Same-station transfer or station change? Don't mix them up."

3. Too tight? Have it suggest backups: "Miss it and switch to this one — smallest loss."

**Real talk:** Rebooking happens in the 12306 app (China's official rail ticketing app), by you. It does the timing math and the backups.

</details>

<a id="skill-353"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>353. International SIM Cards</b> —— Land with no data — can't even hail a ride…</summary>

You land and realize you have no data. Can't even hail a ride. Just standing there.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your destination and days: "Compare eSIM, local SIM, and roaming packs — which wins?"

2. Have it list where to buy: "Buy ahead or on arrival? Don't get ripped off."

3. Have it write the setup steps: "How to activate the eSIM, step by step."

**Real talk:** It can't buy the card for you — ordering, ID verification, and activation are all yours.

</details>

<a id="skill-354"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>354. Paying Abroad</b> —— Alipay dead abroad, no cash — can't even eat…</summary>

You arrive abroad and Alipay doesn't work. No cash exchanged either. Even eating becomes a problem.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your destination: "Check local payments — cards, QR, cash. Which is most universal?"

2. Have it list the currency-exchange playbook: "How much, where — not at the airport."

3. Have it prep a Plan B: "Card gets locked — have the backup ready first."

**Real talk:** Exchanging currency and linking cards happen in your bank app, by you. It only gives the playbook.

</details>

<a id="skill-355"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>355. Travel Insurance</b> —— Pages of terms — "not covered" when it counts…</summary>

The travel insurance has pages of terms. When something actually happens, you learn this isn't covered and that isn't either.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your destination and activities: "For high-risk stuff like diving and skiing — does basic coverage pay out?"

2. Have it compare 3 policies: "Coverage, deductibles, rescue hotline — one table, all clear."

3. Have it flag the exclusions: "These lines don't pay — weigh them yourself."

**Real talk:** It can't buy the insurance for you — purchasing and claims go through official channels, by you.

</details>

<a id="skill-356"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>356. Guesthouse Message Scripts</b> —— Talking past the host — midnight, no bed…</summary>

You and the guesthouse host talk past each other. Check-in time never gets settled, and you're on the street at midnight.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your needs: "Draft an inquiry — check-in time, extra bed, parking. Ask everything at once."

2. Have it prep an English version: "Staying abroad — copy, paste, done."

3. If there's a dispute, have it draft the negotiation: "Firm but civil — higher refund odds."

**Real talk:** It writes the scripts, but you message the host, haggle, and complain yourself. It can't send messages for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-357"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>357. Restaurant Reservations</b> —— Two hours in line — mid food, dead tired…</summary>

Two hours in line at the hyped restaurant, and the food is just okay. Exhausted for nothing.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse the city and your tastes: "Find 3 spots locals go — not all tourist traps."

2. Have it draft the reservation message: "Party size, time, dietary restrictions — say it all at once."

3. Have it check hours: "Don't show up to a closed door — lots of places shut on holidays."

**Real talk:** The calls and the waitlist are yours to make. It can't hold your table.

</details>

<a id="skill-358"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>358. Attraction Tickets</b> —— Sold out two weeks early — you stare at crowds…</summary>

Popular attraction tickets sell out two weeks early. You arrive and can only stare at the sea of people.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your destination: "List the must-sees — which need advance booking, how early, which you can buy on-site."

2. Have it build a ticket-buying timeline: "Grab this one Friday, that one three days out — don't miss the windows."

3. Have it find 2 backups: "If the headliner falls through, the backup photographs just as well."

**Real talk:** Snagging tickets takes your own fast fingers — it can't log in and grab them for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-359"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>359. Packing List</b> —— Overpack the useless, forget the essentials…</summary>

Every trip you overpack things you never touch — and leave the essentials at home.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your destination, days, and weather: "Packing list in four buckets — documents, clothes, toiletries, electronics."

2. Have it ruthlessly trim by trip length: "If you can skip it, skip it — lighter bags, freer you."

3. Check off before departure: "Tick every box before you leave — halves the odds of forgetting."

**Real talk:** Listing and trimming are its job. But checking the boxes and zipping the suitcase — that's you. Don't blame the list for forgotten things.

</details>

<a id="skill-360"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>360. Photo Spot Guide</b> —— Same famous city — your photos lose by a mile…</summary>

You visit the famous city, and your photos look ten streets worse than everyone else's.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your destination: "Pull 10 photogenic spots — when to go, where to stand, what to wear."

2. Have it mark the timing: "An hour around sunrise and sunset — best light."

3. Have it advise on outfits: "What color pops most at this spot?"

**Real talk:** Spots and timing are its job. But being at the viewpoint on time and pressing the shutter — that's you. It can't watch the sunrise for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-361"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>361. Budget Travel Planning</b> —— Money runs out halfway — bread for the rest…</summary>

You plan a budget trip, run out of money halfway, and eat bread for the rest of it.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your destination and days: "Budget plan — hostels, street food. Daily spend?"

2. Have it find the money-saving tricks: "This transit card, that free attraction — cuts costs in half."

3. Log spending daily and ask it: "How much over today? How do the remaining days make it back?"

**Real talk:** Budgets and tricks are its job. But logging daily and keeping your hand off the wallet — that's you. The savings land in your pocket.

</details>

<a id="skill-362"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>362. Anniversary Trip</b> —— Wanted romance — lost to the place downstairs…</summary>

You wanted some ceremony for the anniversary, but the restaurant you booked lost to the one downstairs.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse the anniversary and budget: "Recommend 3 plans — city, hotel, surprise moment."

2. Have it script the surprise: "When the flowers arrive, when the cake comes — don't blow the cover."

3. Have it prep a Plan B: "Raining? Move it indoors — the romance stays."

**Real talk:** Ordering flowers, booking restaurants, setting up surprises — all you. It only does the planning.

</details>

<a id="skill-363"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>363. Business Trip Planning</b> —— Five meetings — the expense report is the hard part…</summary>

Three days on the road, five meetings — and back home, the expense report turns out harder than the meeting notes.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your meeting times and places: "String the trip together — flights, hotel, meetings, one line."

2. Have it organize the reimbursement pack: "Invoices, itineraries — ready to paste on return."

3. Have it draft a trip summary template: "Who you saw, what got decided — write it the moment you land."

**Real talk:** Booking tickets and hotels, getting invoices — all you. It does the planning and the paperwork.

</details>

<a id="skill-364"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>364. Pre-Departure Sweep</b> —— Something always left behind — shipping costs more…</summary>

Every trip back, you discover something left at the hotel. Shipping it back costs more than the thing itself.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Before departure, have Muse list a checkout checklist: "Charger, documents, toiletry bag — one by one."

2. Have it order by room zone: "Bathroom, nightstand, closet — sweep each as you go."

3. Check against it at home: "Something missing? Contact the hotel immediately — sooner is better."

**Real talk:** The sweep is yours to walk. It only makes the list — it can't find your forgotten things for you.

</details>

---

## 🏃 Health & Fitness

> Watch data, sleep, making sense of lab reports

<a id="skill-365"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>365. Sleep Data, Decoded</b> —— 7 hours a night, still groggy — data is all numbers…</summary>

You sleep seven hours a night and still feel groggy. Your tracker is full of numbers, and you can't tell what's wrong.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Connect Muse to your Apple Health or Android Health Connect data.

2. Tell it: "Look at my last two weeks of sleep — is my deep sleep enough? What's my worst bedtime?"

3. Have it send you a weekly sleep summary — it nags you on its own after consecutive late nights.

**Real talk:** Connecting Apple Health takes your own tap — it can't reach your phone. The analysis is its job, but actually sleeping early is yours.

</details>

<a id="skill-366"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>366. Weekly Workout Report</b> —— Three months running — any progress? No idea…</summary>

Three months of running, and you can't tell whether you've improved at all.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Let Muse read your fitness data (connect Apple Health or Android Health Connect first).

2. Tell it: "Every Monday morning, send me a workout report — mileage, pace trends, any slacking."

3. Two weeks of decline in a row? Have it adjust next week's training load — and remind you not to force extra miles.

**Real talk:** Connecting the health data takes your own authorization. The report is its job, but the running takes your own legs — it can't run those 5K for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-367"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>367. Hydration Reminders</b> —— Too busy to drink — headache by night, cup still full…</summary>

Get busy and you forget water all day. By evening your head aches — and the cup is still full.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "Remind me to drink water every 2 hours on workdays."

2. Have it set the reminders as scheduled tasks — one extra at lunch, one before clocking out.

3. Each week, have it tally how many you actually drank — miss the target and next week's nudges get pushier.

**Real talk:** The three steps are all its job. But you lift the cup yourself — it can nag a hundred times, and if you don't drink, nothing changes.

</details>

<a id="skill-368"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>368. Checkup Report, Translated</b> —— Arrows and abbreviations — all you got was "recheck"…</summary>

The checkup report is full of arrows and English abbreviations. All you remember is the word "recheck" — and you're panicking.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph your checkup report and send it to Muse.

2. Tell it: "Explain the flagged metrics in plain language — which matter, which are just slightly off."

3. Have it list "things to watch before your next checkup" — saved to long-term memory.

**Real talk:** Muse isn't a doctor — this is reference only, not medical advice. For red-flagged metrics, take the report to a real doctor yourself. It can't diagnose you.

</details>

<a id="skill-369"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>369. Stand-Up Reminders</b> —— One sitting, one afternoon — stiff neck, dark outside…</summary>

You sit for the entire afternoon — stiff neck, aching back. By the time you stand up, it's nearly dark.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "Remind me to stand up and move for 5 minutes every 50 minutes on workdays."

2. Have it set the reminders as scheduled tasks — auto-skipping lunch break.

3. Each evening, have it count your stand-ups — three still days in a row and the nudges get creative.

**Real talk:** It does all three steps itself, nagging on schedule. But the standing up — only your legs can do that.

</details>

<a id="skill-370"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>370. Medication Reminders</b> —— Three doses a day — did I take it? Can't remember…</summary>

Three doses a day, and right after taking one you forget whether you did. Too scared to double up, too scared to skip.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse the drug name, doses per day, and whether it's before or after meals.

2. Tell it: "Remind me at dose time — every time I reply 'took it,' log it."

3. Miss more than twice? Have it remind you to ask your doctor about catching up — don't decide alone.

**Real talk:** Reference only, not medical advice. Starting, stopping, or adjusting meds — that's between you and your doctor. Its word doesn't count.

</details>

<a id="skill-371"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>371. Post-Workout Stretching</b> —— Sit down right after a run — stairs kill you tomorrow…</summary>

You sit straight down after a run. Next day your legs hurt so much you can't do stairs.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse what workout you just did and which muscles are sore.

2. Tell it: "Give me a 10-minute stretch routine, with cues for each move."

3. Have it order the sequence — play the voice walkthrough rep by rep as you go.

**Real talk:** Whether your form is right — check yourself in the mirror. It can't see you.

</details>

<a id="skill-372"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>372. Form Checks</b> —— Knees cave in on squats — six months, more hurt…</summary>

Your knees cave in on squats. Six months of compensating — the more you train, the more you hurt.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Have a friend film your sets from the side and front.

2. Send Muse screenshots: "Tell me what's wrong with my squat."

3. Have it list the 3 biggest fixes, plus cue words to correct them.

**Real talk:** It only reads pictures. Going heavy — have a spotter with you. Don't tough it out alone.

</details>

<a id="skill-373"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>373. Muscle-Gain Meal Plan</b> —— Lifting daily, scale frozen — protein's too low…</summary>

You lift every day and the scale won't budge. You're simply not eating enough protein.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your height, weight, and training frequency.

2. Tell it: "At 1.6g protein per kg of bodyweight daily, build me a week of muscle-gain meals."

3. Have it swap every meal's ingredients for what you actually buy — nothing exotic.

**Real talk:** Cooking and eating are still yours. It does the math and the pairings.

</details>

<a id="skill-374"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>374. Breaking a Fat-Loss Plateau</b> —— Down 10 pounds — then the scale welds shut…</summary>

You're down 10 pounds, then the scale welds shut. Eating even less won't move it.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your last two weeks of weight, food, and workout logs.

2. Tell it: "Figure out why I plateaued — give me three ways to break through."

3. Have it set a two-week experiment — log your weight daily and watch the trend.

**Real talk:** The weighing and logging are yours to do by hand. It only analyzes what you give it.

</details>

<a id="skill-375"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>375. Marathon Training Plan</b> —— Marathon in 6 months — mileage and pacing a mystery…</summary>

You signed up for a marathon six months out. How to build mileage, how to set pace — no clue.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your current monthly mileage, best 10K, and goal finish time.

2. Tell it: "Give me a 16-week plan — weekly mileage, workouts, rest days, all listed."

3. Send it your actual mileage weekly — it fine-tunes next week off your completion rate.

**Real talk:** Lacing up and stacking the miles — all you. It only writes the plan.

</details>

<a id="skill-376"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>376. Swim Training Log</b> —— Three months in — still gassed at 50 meters…</summary>

Three months of swimming and you're still gassed at 50 meters. You can't see your own stroke flaws.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. After each swim, log the distance, sets, and how it felt — send it to Muse.

2. Tell it: "Organize this into a training log — flag my slowest-improving item."

3. Have it design next week's drills for the weak spot — say, kick-focused sets.

**Real talk:** You're the one in the water. It only reviews and plans.

</details>

<a id="skill-377"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>377. Cycling Route Planning</b> —— Weekend ride — routes by hearsay, climbs unknown…</summary>

You want a weekend ride, but routes come from asking cycling buddies — and you have no read on the climbs.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your start point, target distance, and fitness level.

2. Tell it: "Plan a 60 km loop — avoid roads packed with trucks."

3. Have it mark supply stops and climbs, and generate a route card for your phone.

**Real talk:** It can't verify road conditions live — check navigation yourself before you roll out.

</details>

<a id="skill-378"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>378. Learning Yoga Poses</b> —— Following videos — guessing if your form's right…</summary>

You follow yoga videos, but whether your poses are right is pure guesswork.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your flexibility level and the pose you want — say, pigeon pose.

2. Tell it: "List the entry steps, breathing cues, and common mistakes for this pose."

3. Have it build a 4-week progression — from the easy version to the full pose.

**Real talk:** Joint pain? Stop immediately. Only you know what your body feels.

</details>

<a id="skill-379"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>379. Body Composition Report, Decoded</b> —— Body-fat %, visceral fat — all Greek to you…</summary>

The body composition report is all numbers. Body-fat percentage, visceral fat — you don't know what any of it means.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph your report and send it to Muse.

2. Tell it: "Explain each metric in plain language — and flag the two I should fix first."

3. Have it set three doable mini-goals from the data — say, 3,000 extra steps a day.

**Real talk:** It reads reports, not diagnoses. Real red flags go to a doctor.

</details>

<a id="skill-380"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>380. Tracking Abnormal Checkup Metrics</b> —— Flagged every year — same as last year, ignored…</summary>

Every year's checkup has flagged metrics. Whatever was off last year is still off this year — nobody's tracking it.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your last three years of checkup reports.

2. Tell it: "Table the abnormal metrics by year — flag what's been getting worse."

3. Have it generate a question list — take it to the doctor and miss nothing.

**Real talk:** It only organizes the trend. What the metrics mean and whether to treat — the doctor decides.

</details>

<a id="skill-381"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>381. Snore Tracking</b> —— Snoring like thunder — and you have no idea…</summary>

Everyone says you snore like thunder. You have no idea what's actually going on.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Record a night with a recording app — send Muse the audio or your snore app's report.

2. Tell it: "Analyze when the snoring peaks and what might trigger it."

3. Have it list fixes: side-sleeping, losing weight, no alcohol before bed.

**Real talk:** Daytime drowsy, breathing pauses? You need an in-person sleep study at a hospital.

</details>

<a id="skill-382"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>382. Neck Relief Routine</b> —— Head down all day — neck's a brick that cracks…</summary>

Head down all day and your neck turns to a brick. Turning it cracks.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Describe your symptoms to Muse: stiffness, pain, or numb hands.

2. Tell it: "Give me a desk-friendly neck routine, with a duration for each move."

3. Have it remind you daily to get up and move for 5 minutes.

**Real talk:** Numb hands, worsening dizziness? Stop DIYing — go see a doctor.

</details>

<a id="skill-383"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>383. Back Pain Journal</b> —— Pain comes and goes — can't explain it at the doctor's…</summary>

Your back pain comes and goes. At the doctor's, you can't say where it hurts or for how long.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Each time it hurts, log the time, spot, intensity, and what you were doing — send it to Muse.

2. Tell it: "Organize this into a pain journal — look for patterns."

3. Have it generate a one-page visit summary — how long, what aggravates it. Hand it to the doctor.

**Real talk:** It only logs and organizes. Diagnosis and treatment plans come from a doctor.

</details>

<a id="skill-384"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>384. Dry Eye Relief</b> —— Screens all afternoon — eyes feel full of sand…</summary>

Screens until the afternoon, and your eyes feel like they're full of sand.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your screen habits: hours per day, contacts or not.

2. Tell it: "Give me a daily dry-eye routine."

3. Have it remind you every 45 minutes to do the 20-20-20 rule: 20 feet away, 20 seconds.

**Real talk:** Read the label before using eye drops. Noticeably worse vision? See an eye doctor.

</details>

<a id="skill-385"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>385. Dental Health Tracking</b> —— Dentist only when it hurts — brushing unchecked…</summary>

You only think of the dentist when a tooth aches. Whether you brush right the rest of the time — nobody's watching.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your dental situation: cavities, bleeding gums or not.

2. Tell it: "Build me a dental plan — brushing, flossing, cleaning schedule."

3. Have it remind you: cleanings every six months, a full checkup yearly.

**Real talk:** Toothache, gums that won't stop bleeding — don't wait it out. See a dentist.

</details>

<a id="skill-386"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>386. Sunscreen Basics</b> —— Shelves of sunscreen — cloudy-day rules all vibes…</summary>

You've bought piles of sunscreen, but whether to apply on cloudy days or reapply — pure mood.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your skin type and daily commute.

2. Tell it: "Explain sunscreen properly — how to pick SPF, how much, how often to reapply."

3. Have it build reminders around your schedule: before heading out, after lunch.

**Real talk:** Already sunburned and blistering, or a mole that's changing — see a dermatologist.

</details>

<a id="skill-387"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>387. Quit-Smoking Tracker</b> —— Quit 800 times — every cigarette "the last one"…</summary>

You've quit eight hundred times. Every cigarette is "the last one."

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse how many you smoke daily and which moments break you.

2. Tell it: "Build me a 28-day quit plan — flag the hardest days."

3. Log each craving daily — it reviews with you and adjusts the strategy.

**Real talk:** You resist each cigarette yourself. It only keeps score and cheers you on.

</details>

<a id="skill-388"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>388. Blood-Sugar-Friendly Eating</b> —— Sugar's high, doctor says cut back — what's allowed?…</summary>

Your checkup shows high blood sugar. The doctor says watch the sugar, but you have no idea what you can eat.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your glucose numbers and your usual three meals.

2. Tell it: "By blood-sugar rules, give me three lists — eat freely, eat less, don't touch."

3. Have it rebuild your regular takeout orders into low-GI versions.

**Real talk:** These are food suggestions, not medical orders. Meds and follow-ups — listen to your doctor.

</details>

<a id="skill-389"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>389. Gout Diet Guide</b> —— Uric acid high — hotpot's out, but what can you eat?…</summary>

High uric acid. Hotpot and seafood are off-limits, but you have no reliable sense of what you can eat.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your uric acid level and flare history.

2. Tell it: "Build a gout food traffic light — green means go, red means don't."

3. Have it run through your favorite dishes — flag the high-purine ones with swaps.

**Real talk:** Mid-flare and can't walk? Don't tough it out — see a doctor.

</details>

<a id="skill-390"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>390. Blood-Pressure-Friendly Eating</b> —— Doctor says less salt — but how much is that?…</summary>

Your blood pressure runs high. The doctor says cut salt, but you have no feel for your daily grams.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your readings and your taste (heavy-handed or light).

2. Tell it: "By DASH diet rules, plan me a week of low-salt meals."

3. Have it teach you to read nutrition labels — spot the sodium traps at a glance.

**Real talk:** Whether to take meds and how to adjust them — only your doctor decides. It handles the food.

</details>

<a id="skill-391"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>391. Cholesterol Management</b> —— LDL's high — beyond "eat less grease," what else?…</summary>

Your checkup shows high LDL. Beyond "eat less greasy food," you don't know what else to do.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your four lipid-panel numbers.

2. Tell it: "Explain these four in plain language — then give me an actionable improvement list."

3. Have it nudge you monthly: diet, exercise, weight — three check-ins.

**Real talk:** Statins or not — that call belongs to your doctor. It only manages lifestyle.

</details>

<a id="skill-392"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>392. Picking a Checkup Package</b> —— Hundreds to thousands — scam or gap, pick your fear…</summary>

Checkup packages run from a few hundred to tens of thousands. The pricey ones might be a scam; the cheap ones might miss things.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your age, sex, family history, and budget.

2. Tell it: "Compare these packages — which tests are must-haves, which are a waste of money."

3. Have it generate a buying list — take it and order without second-guessing.

**Real talk:** Ordering and paying happen at the clinic, by you. It only advises.

</details>

<a id="skill-393"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>393. Adult Vaccine Prep</b> —— HPV, flu, shingles — advice from Xiaohongshu?…</summary>

HPV, flu, shingles — what adults should get, you're learning from Xiaohongshu.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your age, sex, and which vaccines you've had.

2. Tell it: "Per official guidelines, list the vaccines recommended for my age group."

3. Have it turn that into a consultation checklist — go down it item by item at the community clinic.

**Real talk:** Whether you can get a shot and which one — the vaccinating doctor decides in person. It only does your homework.

</details>

<a id="skill-394"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>394. Blood Donation Log</b> —— Want to donate — dread the hassle, no record after…</summary>

You want to donate blood but dread the hassle — and afterward there's no record of what it meant for your body.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "I want to donate blood — walk me through the process, requirements, and precautions."

2. Have it find nearby donation sites and hours, plus a prep checklist.

3. After donating, log the donor card number — have it file it and remind you yearly.

**Real talk:** The exam and the draw happen in person, by you. It only does the research.

</details>

<a id="skill-395"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>395. TCM Constitution Self-Check</b> —— Everyone says "damp-heat" — but what are you really?…</summary>

People keep telling you you're a "damp-heat constitution." What you actually are — you can't say.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Describe your typical symptoms to Muse: run cold or hot, what your tongue coating looks like.

2. Tell it: "Using the nine-constitution system, give me a preliminary read on which one I lean toward."

3. Have it list daily-life and diet tuning tips for that constitution.

**Real talk:** This is self-check reference only — for actual treatment, see a licensed TCM doctor in person.

</details>

<a id="skill-396"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>396. Massage Shop Smarts</b> —— Achy back, scary hacks — how to pick a safe shop?…</summary>

Your back aches and you want a massage — but you're scared of a hack who makes it worse.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse what you want fixed and your budget.

2. Tell it: "Teach me a few moves — how to pick a legit shop, which sales pitches are traps."

3. Have it list the hard no-gos: conditions where you absolutely must not get massaged.

**Real talk:** Uncomfortable mid-massage? Speak up immediately. It's your body — don't be shy.

</details>

<a id="skill-397"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>397. Stress Venting</b> —— Too stressed to sleep — telling anyone feels dramatic…</summary>

You're too stressed to sleep, but telling anyone feels like being dramatic.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse what's been weighing on you, one thing at a time.

2. Tell it: "Talk it through with me — help me get the feelings out first, then let's problem-solve."

3. Have it teach you a few instant calm-downs: breathing, body scans.

**Real talk:** It can only listen and keep you company. Feeling low for more than two weeks straight — see a therapist.

</details>

<a id="skill-398"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>398. Sleep Hygiene Reset</b> —— Exhausted but scrolling till 2am — coffee tomorrow…</summary>

You're exhausted but scroll till 2am. Next day you run on coffee.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your real schedule and bedtime habits — honestly.

2. Tell it: "By sleep-hygiene rules, build me a one-hour pre-bed routine."

3. Have it remind you at 11pm to put the phone down and run the routine.

**Real talk:** Chronic insomnia, severe daytime drowsiness — don't tough it out. See a doctor.

</details>

---

## 📁 Files & Cloud Drive

> Find files, organize photos, contracts and invoices

<a id="skill-399"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>399. Sort phone photos by event</b> —— 8,000 photos on your phone, and finding last year's team shot takes half an hour.</summary>

You've got 8,000 photos on your phone, and finding last year's team-building group shot takes half an hour of scrolling.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Export the photos and send them to Muse, or let it read the photo folder on your computer directly.

2. Tell it: "Sort into folders by event and date — trips, parties, work screenshots, each in their own place."

3. When it's done, have it list everything out, flag duplicates and blurry shots, and only delete after you confirm.

**Real talk:** It'll handle all the sorting and filing, but step 3 — deleting photos — has to be your call. Delete the wrong one and it's gone for good; it won't make that decision for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-400"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>400. Clean up duplicate files</b> —— Hard drive in the red, five versions of the same installer sitting in Downloads.</summary>

Your hard drive is in the red, and there are five versions of the same installer sitting in your Downloads folder.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Have Muse scan the folder you point it to.

2. Tell it: "Find files with identical content but different names, and show me the list."

3. Check off what you want gone and it'll clear them in one go — back things up first, and don't nuke anything important in one click.

**Real talk:** It does all the scanning and matching, but step 3 — picking what to delete — is on you. It can't tell "looks useless" from "actually useless"; if it deletes the wrong thing, that's on you.

</details>

<a id="skill-401"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>401. Pull key terms out of contracts</b> —— A dozen-page rental contract, and you have no idea which clause covers the deposit.</summary>

Your rental contract is a dozen pages long, and after signing you still don't know which clause covers getting your deposit back.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send the contract PDF to Muse.

2. Tell it: "Pull the key terms — amounts, duration, penalties, termination conditions — into a table."

3. Have it flag anything that works against you, then take that list into the negotiation before you sign.

**Real talk:** It handles the reading and red flags, but step 3 — sitting down to negotiate — is all you. It can't sign for you, and it definitely can't argue for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-402"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>402. Batch-rename invoices</b> —— Reimbursement season, screen full of "e-invoice.pdf" — renaming one by one makes you want to quit.</summary>

It's reimbursement season, your screen is full of files named "e-invoice.pdf," and renaming them one by one makes you want to quit.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Dump the pile of invoice PDFs on Muse.

2. Tell it: "Rename them as date-merchant-amount, and set aside any where the amount doesn't match."

3. Have it generate a reimbursement summary you can paste straight into the expense system.

**Real talk:** It does all the renaming and reconciling, but step 3 — pasting into the expense system and submitting — is yours. It can't get into your company's internal OA system.

</details>

<a id="skill-403"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>403. Cloud-share link expiry reminders</b> —— A shared cloud link expired and you only found out when someone chased you.</summary>

A cloud-drive link you shared expired, and you only found out when someone chased you about it. Resending everything is awkward.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Give Muse the share links and their expiry dates, and have it log them as scheduled tasks.

2. Tell it: "Remind me two days before expiry, and tell me how many times I've renewed this one."

3. When the reminder comes, renew the link in your cloud drive yourself, then report the new date back to it.

**Real talk:** Cloud drives don't offer an open API, so Muse can only track expiry dates for you — it can't check them automatically. The renewal step is yours.

</details>

<a id="skill-404"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>404. New-phone data migration checklist</b> —— Moved the photos, forgot the chat history — remembered only after selling the old phone.</summary>

You moved the photos but forgot the chat history, and only remembered after selling the old phone.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "I'm switching phones — make me a data migration checklist."

2. Have it sort by category — photos, contacts, chat history, app accounts, authenticator apps — with how-to for each.

3. Check items off as you go, and only let it remind you to factory-reset the old phone when everything's done.

**Real talk:** It makes the list and the how-tos; moving photos and chats, and the factory reset — every step is hands-on for you. It can't touch either of your phones.

</details>

<a id="skill-405"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>405. Phone photo backup plan</b> —— 128GB phone is full — deleting photos hurts, but there's no room left.</summary>

Your 128GB phone is full. Deleting photos hurts, but there's no space left.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your phone model and roughly how many GB of photos you have.

2. Say: "Compare backup options for me — cloud drive, NAS, external hard drive — which fits my situation?"

3. Have it write up the steps, and only delete originals from your phone after the backup's done.

**Real talk:** Plugging in cables and scanning login codes — that's all you.

</details>

<a id="skill-406"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>406. Rescue expired WeChat files</b> —— A client sent a file three days ago — you open it and it's "expired."</summary>

A client sent you a file three days ago. You open it and it says "expired." Your heart sinks.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot the chat and send it to Muse, noting the file type and when it was sent.

2. Say: "Walk me through every rescue option — forwarded records, desktop cache, asking them to resend."

3. Have it set a house rule for you: important files get saved the moment they arrive, no more expiries.

**Real talk:** If the file's truly expired, only the sender can resend it — it can't conjure it back.

</details>

<a id="skill-407"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>407. Clean up cloud drive space</b> —— 2TB cloud drive is full — renew or clean up? You've been going back and forth.</summary>

Your 2TB cloud drive is full. Renew or clean up? You've been going back and forth for ages.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot the biggest folders in your cloud drive and send them to Muse.

2. Say: "Tell me what I can delete, what I can archive elsewhere, and what's worth keeping."

3. Have it make a cleanup list — work through it, and the space comes back.

**Real talk:** You're the one hitting delete. Back up the important stuff first.

</details>

<a id="skill-408"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>408. Clear out near-duplicate phone photos</b> —— Burst-shot a dozen to get one good one — the rest just sit in your album.</summary>

You burst-shot a dozen photos to get one good one, and the rest are just sitting in your album.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Ask Muse: "Teach me to filter similar photos with my phone's built-in album features."

2. Have it set your rules: blurry goes, eyes-closed goes, same composition keeps only one.

3. Have it remind you monthly — 15 minutes and you're done.

**Real talk:** Which one to keep comes down to your eye — it can't decide for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-409"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>409. Sort your screenshots</b> —— 3,000 screenshots in your album — finding one chat record from last year takes half an hour.</summary>

There are 3,000 screenshots in your album. Finding one chat record from last year takes half an hour.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse what your screenshots are for: to-dos, ideas, receipts, funny stuff.

2. Say: "Design me a screenshot folder naming system."

3. Spend 10 minutes a week filing by the system, and have it remind you not to let them pile up.

**Real talk:** You're the one doing the filing — it just designs the system.

</details>

<a id="skill-410"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>410. Merge and split PDFs</b> —— A dozen PDFs to combine into one — copying one by one is soul-crushing.</summary>

You've got a dozen PDFs to combine into one for a deliverable, and copying them one by one is soul-crushing.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. List the PDFs for Muse and say whether you're merging or splitting.

2. Say: "Recommend a free tool and walk me through merging in three steps."

3. Have it write the steps down as a note — next time, just follow it.

**Real talk:** You have to download and install the software yourself — it can only tell you where to click.

</details>

<a id="skill-411"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>411. Extract text from scans</b> —— Paper contract scanned as images — copying one paragraph means typing it by hand.</summary>

Your paper contract is scanned as images, and copying a single paragraph means typing it out by hand.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send the scans to Muse.

2. Say: "Extract the text and clean it up into a copyable document."

3. Have it proofread — blurry characters get flagged for you to check by hand.

**Real talk:** You'll need to verify misread characters yourself — don't use the output blind.

</details>

<a id="skill-412"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>412. Back up ID photos to the cloud</b> —— Need an ID photo for a visa — can't find one anywhere, end up retaking it.</summary>

You need an ID photo for a visa application, can't find one anywhere on your phone, and end up retaking it.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph your ID card, passport, and ID photos in each size, and send them to Muse.

2. Say: "Build me an ID-photo archive: categorized, named, saved to a designated cloud folder."

3. Have it generate a checklist: which sizes you're missing, which ones are expiring.

**Real talk:** Photographing and uploading is on you — it handles the planning and reminders.

</details>

<a id="skill-413"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>413. Scan and archive old photos</b> —— The family albums are yellowing and cracking — digitize now or they're gone.</summary>

The old family albums are yellowing and cracking. If you don't digitize them now, they're gone for good.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Ask Muse: "Teach me to photograph old photos well with my phone — lighting, angles, killing glare."

2. Have it set a naming rule: year-person-event, so nothing gets lost.

3. Once scanned, keep a copy in the cloud and put one on a hard drive for your parents.

**Real talk:** Photographing and scanning is one-by-one, all you — it only teaches the method and the rules.

</details>

<a id="skill-414"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>414. Manage scanned contracts</b> —— Rental and work contracts all on paper — finding one means tearing the place apart.</summary>

Your rental and work contracts are all on paper. Finding one means tearing the place apart.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph or scan the paper contracts and send them to Muse.

2. Say: "Pull the key info — parties, amounts, expiry dates — into a ledger."

3. Have it sort by expiry date and remind you a month before anything lapses.

**Real talk:** Scanning and uploading is yours — it just keeps the ledger.

</details>

<a id="skill-415"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>415. Manage résumé versions</b> —— A dozen revisions in — you've lost track of which one you actually sent out.</summary>

You're a dozen revisions deep and you've lost track of which version you actually sent out.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send all the versions to Muse.

2. Say: "Set me a version naming rule: date-company-role."

3. Have it build an application log: which version went where, and what came back.

**Real talk:** Renaming and filing is hands-on for you — it just sets the rules.

</details>

<a id="skill-416"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>416. Build a paper reference library</b> —— Downloaded 50 papers for your thesis — can't find a single one when you need it.</summary>

You downloaded 50 papers for your thesis. When you actually need one, you can't find a single one.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send the paper PDFs to Muse.

2. Say: "Sort by topic, pull each one's core argument into an index table."

3. Have it generate a citation list — copy the format straight into your thesis.

**Real talk:** Downloading and organizing is on you — it just builds the index.

</details>

<a id="skill-417"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>417. Organize your ebooks</b> —— 200 books on the Kindle — the one you want is never findable.</summary>

200 books on the Kindle, and the one you actually want to read is never findable.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Export your book list and send it to Muse.

2. Say: "Re-sort by topic and reading priority, and flag duplicates."

3. Have it make a "12 must-reads this year" list and pin it to the front of your shelf.

**Real talk:** Exporting the list and deleting books is on you — it just does the planning.

</details>

<a id="skill-418"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>418. Organize your music</b> —— 3,000 songs in your playlists — finding the right one is pure shuffle luck.</summary>

3,000 songs in your playlists, and finding the one you're in the mood for is pure shuffle luck.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot or export your playlists and send them to Muse.

2. Say: "Sort by scenario: commute, workout, late night, deep work."

3. Have it pick 20 per scenario and name the new playlists.

**Real talk:** Building playlists and dragging songs — that's you clicking around in the app.

</details>

<a id="skill-419"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>419. Archive your video footage</b> —— 500GB of vlog footage piled up — finding B-roll at edit time makes you want to cry.</summary>

500GB of vlog footage piled up, and finding B-roll at edit time makes you want to cry.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot your footage folder structure and send it to Muse.

2. Say: "Design me an archive system: date-location-content."

3. Have it make a tagging checklist — flag the key clips so you can pull them straight into the edit.

**Real talk:** Renaming and moving files is yours — it just sets the rules.

</details>

<a id="skill-420"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>420. Large file transfer plan</b> —— A 2GB video for a client — WeChat crushes the quality, email won't send it.</summary>

A 2GB video for a client — WeChat crushes the quality, and email won't send it at all.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse how big the file is and what device they're on.

2. Say: "Compare ways to move big files — cloud link, LAN transfer, mailing a drive."

3. Have it write a send guide the client can open and download from.

**Real talk:** Uploading and sending the link is on you — it just picks the plan.

</details>

<a id="skill-421"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>421. Rescue files from cloud trash</b> —— Deleted a bid document from your cloud drive by accident — cold sweat.</summary>

You deleted a bid document from your cloud drive by accident. Cold sweat.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Don't panic. Tell Muse: "I deleted a cloud file by accident — walk me to the trash to get it back."

2. Have it guide you step by step: open trash, filter by date, restore.

3. Once it's back, have it set a house rule: important files go to a "pending delete" zone for 30 days first.

**Real talk:** You're the one hitting restore — and past the trash retention window, not even magic saves it.

</details>

<a id="skill-422"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>422. Fix cloud sync conflicts</b> —— Edited a doc on phone and computer at once — two versions, no idea which to keep.</summary>

You edited a doc on your phone and computer at the same time. Now there are two synced versions and no idea which to keep.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send both conflicting versions to Muse.

2. Say: "Compare the two — highlight what each one added."

3. Have it merge them into one final version, then teach you how to avoid the next conflict.

**Real talk:** Which version survives is your call — it just does the comparing.

</details>

<a id="skill-423"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>423. NAS buying guide for beginners</b> —— Want a NAS for your photos — the models all blur together, no idea what to buy.</summary>

You want a NAS for your photos, but the models all blur together and you have no idea what to buy.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your needs: how much storage, how many users, what budget.

2. Say: "Recommend 2–3 beginner NAS models for my needs, with pros and cons."

3. Have it make a buy-and-setup checklist — unbox and follow it.

**Real talk:** Ordering, slotting in drives, installing the OS — all you.

</details>

<a id="skill-424"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>424. Hard drive health check</b> —— Five-year-old drive full of photos — if it dies one day, everything's gone.</summary>

Your hard drive is five years old and full of photos. If it dies one day, everything's gone.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Ask Muse: "Teach me to check drive health — how do I read SMART data?"

2. Have it set a schedule: check quarterly, screenshot and archive the results.

3. The moment it goes yellow, have it plan your migration — don't wait for red.

**Real talk:** Plugging in the drive and running the diagnostics is on you — it just teaches you to read the results.

</details>

<a id="skill-425"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>425. Back up like you mean it: the 3-2-1 rule</b> —— Computer dies — thesis and photos all gone. You want to cry.</summary>

Your computer dies, and the thesis and photos go with it. You want to cry.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse what file types matter most to you.

2. Say: "Design me a 3-2-1 backup plan — 3 copies, 2 media types, 1 offsite."

3. Have it make an execution checklist with reminders, and verify the backups monthly.

**Real talk:** Copying files and buying drives is hands-on for you — it just does the planning.

</details>

<a id="skill-426"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>426. Move to a password manager</b> —— All your passwords live in Notes — lose the phone, lose everything.</summary>

All your passwords live in your Notes app. Lose the phone, lose everything.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "I want to move from Notes to a password manager — recommend one and teach me the move."

2. Have it list the steps: export, import, turn on two-factor.

3. Have it audit your weak passwords into a list, and change them one by one.

**Real talk:** The master password is yours to set and memorize — it won't remember it for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-427"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>427. Tidy your browser bookmarks</b> —— 500 links crammed in the bookmark bar — finding a daily one takes three minutes.</summary>

500 links crammed in your bookmark bar. Finding a daily one takes three minutes.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Export your bookmarks and send them to Muse.

2. Say: "Sort by how often I use them: daily, occasional, deletable."

3. Have it design a new folder structure — then reorganize once, following it.

**Real talk:** Dragging bookmarks and deleting links — that's you in the browser.

</details>

<a id="skill-428"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>428. Back up WeChat chat history</b> —— Switching phones and terrified of losing chats — key client conversations live there.</summary>

Switching phones, and terrified of losing your chats — key client conversations live in there.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Ask Muse: "Teach me to back up chats with WeChat's built-in migration."

2. Have it list a pre-backup checklist: WiFi, battery, storage space.

3. After the backup, have it set a cadence: back up important threads monthly.

**Real talk:** Scanning the code and tapping migrate — that's you, on both phones, in person.

</details>

<a id="skill-429"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>429. Free up WeChat storage</b> —— WeChat is eating 30GB — your phone nags about space every day.</summary>

WeChat is eating 30GB, and your phone nags about space every single day.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Open WeChat's storage breakdown, screenshot it, and send it to Muse.

2. Say: "See what's hogging space — chat images, videos, or cache?"

3. Have it order the cleanup: cache first, then big files in group chats, back up the important stuff first.

**Real talk:** You're the one hitting clean — just confirm the backup first.

</details>

<a id="skill-430"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>430. Curate your cloud photo memories</b> —— Tens of thousands of photos in the cloud — your kid's milestones are buried in there.</summary>

Tens of thousands of photos in your cloud album, and your kid's growing-up moments are buried in there.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your theme: kid growing up, travels, family gatherings.

2. Say: "Teach me to filter by timeline and pick the 50 most keep-worthy shots per year."

3. Have it draft a yearly memory-book outline — captions included, ready to use.

**Real talk:** Picking photos and tapping favorite is on you — it just sets the bar.

</details>

<a id="skill-431"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>431. Set up a family shared album</b> —— One trip, photos scattered across four phones — good luck stitching them together.</summary>

One family trip, photos scattered across four phones. Good luck stitching them together.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse what phones everyone uses and which cloud service you're on.

2. Say: "Recommend a family shared album even my parents can handle."

3. Have it write a dead-simple tutorial for your parents: how to upload, how to view.

**Real talk:** Creating the album and adding the family — that's you.

</details>

<a id="skill-432"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>432. Design a folder structure that works</b> —— 200 files piled on the desktop — finding anything is pure search luck.</summary>

200 files piled on your desktop. Finding anything is pure search luck.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your file types: what belongs to work, study, life.

2. Say: "Design me a folder tree, no more than three levels deep."

3. Have it make an organizing checklist — one weekend hour, everything in place.

**Real talk:** Dragging files into folders is yours — it just draws the blueprint.

</details>

---

## 🔗 Power Combos

> Chain two tricks together: 1+1 > 2

<a id="skill-433"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>433. Turn booking emails into calendar trips</b> —— Orders sit in your inbox, but flight days and hotel addresses take digging.</summary>

Your booking confirmations sit in your inbox, but which day you fly and where you're staying takes digging — and your calendar's empty.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse: "Find all the booking emails for this trip," and give it your dates and destination.

2. Have it stitch flight numbers, departure times, and hotel addresses into one itinerary, saved to the right dates in Google Calendar.

3. The day before you leave, have it read the whole thing back — it'll flag any leg you're missing.

**Real talk:** It can only find booking emails in Gmail — pickup codes sitting in WeChat are invisible to it. Screenshot those and send them over.

</details>

<a id="skill-434"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>434. Compare prices, then let it watch for drops</b> —— Still pricey after comparing — missing a drop stings worse than overpaying.</summary>

It's still pricey after comparing, and you can't refresh the page every day. Missing a price drop stings worse than overpaying.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. First have Muse compare prices and lock in the model and the current lowest price.

2. Say: "Tell me the second this model drops below X — check once a day for me."

3. When the alert lands, don't hesitate — it tracks public prices, and stock moves fast.

**Real talk:** It watches public list prices — app-only coupons and livestream flash sales are invisible to it. During big sales, keep an eye out yourself too.

</details>

<a id="skill-435"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>435. Turn your health data into a weekly podcast</b> —— Your watch logs data daily — you never look back, and your sleep's been sliding.</summary>

Your watch logs data every day and you never look back. Your sleep's been sliding and you don't know since when.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Have Muse connect to Apple Health or Health Connect, and see what it can read.

2. Say: "Every Sunday night, turn my week's sleep, steps, and workouts into a 3-minute podcast for me."

3. If the numbers slide two weeks running, have it say so plainly — conclusions, not just numbers.

**Real talk:** It can interpret data and suggest — it can't diagnose. If something actually feels wrong, see a doctor yourself. Don't treat the podcast as one.

</details>

<a id="skill-436"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>436. Build a topic bank with daily writing ammo</b> —— Running an account, the hardest part isn't writing — it's the blank doc every morning.</summary>

Running an account, the hardest part isn't writing — it's opening the doc every day with no idea what to write, and forgetting the idea by the time you note it down.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Toss stray ideas at Muse as they come — have it build a topic bank, tagged by niche and heat.

2. Say: "Every morning at 10, pick 3 from the bank with an angle for each, and send them to me."

3. After publishing, send the link back — have it log the numbers. The bank gets sharper over time.

**Real talk:** It gives you ammo, not finished pieces — the post still needs your human voice. Copy its writing verbatim and the AI smell is strong.

</details>

<a id="skill-437"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>437. Let payday log itself and feed your investments</b> —— The salary text gets one glance and it's forgotten — month-end, no idea where it went.</summary>

The salary notification gets one glance and it's forgotten. Month-end, no idea where the money went — and investing happens whenever you remember.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Have Muse watch your payday notifications (bank texts, screenshots, or emails) and log the income the day it lands.

2. Say: "Three days after payday each month, remind me to move the investment money" — you set the amount and the target.

3. Month-end, have it make one table: earned, spent, invested. Crystal clear.

**Real talk:** It can't get into your banking app — you send the pay-stub screenshots. Tapping transfer and buy is forever your finger.

</details>

<a id="skill-438"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>438. Automate the whole meeting flow</b> —— No prep before, no notes during, no follow-up after — a meeting with no paper trail is wasted.</summary>

No prep before, no notes during, no follow-up after — a meeting with no paper trail might as well not have happened.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Before the meeting, have Muse generate a one-page brief from your calendar: who, what was decided last time, what's on the table.

2. After, drop the transcript on it: "Turn this into minutes, push the to-dos into Google Tasks with deadlines."

3. Before the next meeting it auto-reviews open items — overdue to-dos get flagged at you first.

**Real talk:** The transcript has to come from your meeting app — you export, it processes. Calling the shots in the room is always you; it just strings the workflow together.

</details>

<a id="skill-439"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>439. Turn deadline emails into to-dos</b> —— A client email hides a "quote by Friday" — you forget it, and Friday they come asking.</summary>

A client email hides a "quote by Friday." You read it, forget it, and Friday they come asking.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Have Muse scan your inbox every morning for emails with hard deadlines.

2. Say: "Turn these into a to-do list, sorted by deadline, urgent ones in red."

3. Every evening, have it reconcile: what's done, what rolls to tomorrow.

**Real talk:** The inbox needs your authorization first — you tap that yourself. It reads mail, it doesn't reply for you.

</details>

<a id="skill-440"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>440. Turn tomorrow's schedule into a morning podcast</b> —— Five meetings on the calendar — mornings are chaos and you nearly miss the first one.</summary>

Five meetings on the calendar. Mornings are chaos and you nearly miss the first one.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Have Muse connect to your calendar and read all of tomorrow's events.

2. Say: "Make tomorrow's meetings a 2-minute morning podcast: times, topics, what to prep."

3. Listen while you wash up — walk out the door already knowing the day.

**Real talk:** The calendar authorization is yours to tap. It only reads what's scheduled — tell it about last-minute additions yourself.

</details>

<a id="skill-441"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>441. Turn a meeting recording into minutes and to-dos</b> —— An hour-long meeting ends, nobody writes it up, everything's forgotten in three days.</summary>

An hour-long meeting ends, nobody writes it up, and everything agreed is forgotten in three days.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Drop the recording or transcript on Muse — have it structure the minutes first.

2. Say: "Pull every to-do from the minutes: who's on it, due when — as a list."

3. Before the next meeting, have it check the list: what got done, what's still open gets flagged.

**Real talk:** You record and send the audio yourself. For confidential meetings, confirm your company is fine with the audio leaving the building.

</details>

<a id="skill-442"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>442. Turn travel photos into a travelogue</b> —— 300 photos from the trip — back home, they just rot in the album.</summary>

300 photos from the trip. Back home, they just rot in the album.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse the trip's photos day by day — have it sort by time and place first.

2. Say: "From the places and people in the shots, write a vivid travelogue."

3. Add two lines of how you actually felt, plus the funny bits — and you've got a postable story.

**Real talk:** It can't invent the story behind the photos — you tell it the whens, wheres, and whos first. It just strings them into prose.

</details>

<a id="skill-443"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>443. Feed it your résumé, get sharper interview prep</b> —— Asked about a project on your résumé, you fumble — nothing you prepped came up.</summary>

Asked about a project on your résumé, you fumble. Nothing you prepped came up.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your résumé plus the target role's job description (JD).

2. Say: "Play interviewer — grill me with three follow-up questions per project on my résumé."

3. Answer out loud once, have it spot the logic holes, then give you the stronger version.

**Real talk:** It's never interviewed at your company — it drills the generic follow-up playbook. Real questions still come from interview write-ups you find yourself.

</details>

<a id="skill-444"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>444. Screenshot your cart, get the lowest price across the web</b> —— A dozen items in the cart — no idea which is cheaper elsewhere.</summary>

A dozen items in your cart. No idea which one's cheaper elsewhere, and checking one by one takes forever.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot your cart for Muse — have it recognize each item's name and current price.

2. Say: "Check these across the major platforms and build me a comparison table."

3. Pick the big gaps, stack coupons and bundles, then check out.

**Real talk:** It can't check out or pay for you. It checks public listings — verify coupon prices inside the app yourself.

</details>

<a id="skill-445"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>445. Turn a pile of receipts into an expense report</b> —— Back from a trip with a stack of receipts — the report takes till midnight and never ties.</summary>

Back from a business trip with a stack of receipts. Pasting the expense report takes till midnight, and the numbers never tie out.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph the receipts one by one for Muse — have it read the date, amount, and vendor.

2. Say: "Build an expense detail sheet on our company template."

3. Check the amounts once yourself — then print and paste the tickets.

**Real talk:** Photographing is one-by-one, all you. If it misreads an amount, you fix it — the numbers you submit are your responsibility.

</details>

<a id="skill-446"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>446. Let sleep and workout data write next week's training plan</b> —— Running on vibes — crush it or couch it. Training's a mess.</summary>

Running on vibes — crush it when you feel good, couch it when you don't. Your training's a mess.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Have Muse read this week's sleep and workout data.

2. Say: "Based on my recovery this week, plan next week: how many run days, how many rest days."

3. After each session, report how it felt — have it fine-tune the coming days.

**Real talk:** That's general guidance, not a medical diagnosis. Knee pain, chest tightness — those signals mean you see a doctor yourself.

</details>

<a id="skill-447"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>447. Link financial news to your holdings</b> —— By the time you connect a bad headline to your holdings, the stock's done falling.</summary>

You read the news every day. By the time you connect a bad headline to your holdings, the stock's already done falling.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Tell Muse your holdings — have it remember which names you own.

2. Say: "Every evening, pick the news tied to my holdings and explain the impact in one line each."

3. On big bad news, have it walk the logic first — then you decide whether to touch the position.

**Real talk:** Your holdings are what you told it — it can't see your brokerage account. Buy and sell decisions are yours; it just saves you reading time.

</details>

<a id="skill-448"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>448. Flight texts auto-join the calendar, with check-in nudges</b> —— Booking text sinks without a trace — night before, no check-in, no alarm.</summary>

The booking confirmation text sinks without a trace. The night before, you realize you never checked in and never set an alarm.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot the booking text or email for Muse — have it pull the flight number and departure time.

2. Say: "Make calendar events: remind me to leave 3 hours before departure, and to check in 24 hours before."

3. Import the events — the reminders will ping you on time from there.

**Real talk:** Check-in and seat selection are you, inside the airline app. It just does the math and the nudging.

</details>

<a id="skill-449"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>449. Put contract dates on your calendar automatically</b> —— "Notify in writing 30 days before renewal" — you find the clause after it's expired.</summary>

The contract says "notify in writing 30 days before renewal." You find the clause after it's already expired.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Photograph the contract for Muse — have it find every dated clause: expiries, payment days, notice periods.

2. Say: "Turn these into calendar reminders, notice lead-times included."

3. Import to your calendar — it'll ping you ahead of the key dates.

**Real talk:** It can miss clauses when reading contracts. Before signing, re-check the key dates against the original yourself.

</details>

<a id="skill-450"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>450. Turn chat logs into follow-up to-dos</b> —— You promise a client three things in chat — forget two, and the client remembers better.</summary>

You promise a client a pile of things in chat. Turn around and forget which ones — the client remembers better than you do.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Screenshot the client chat and send it to Muse.

2. Say: "Pull everything I promised: what, due when — as a follow-up list."

3. Weekly, have it walk the list with you — anything unclosed gets flagged.

**Real talk:** Screenshotting is on you. Anything involving a price — re-read it yourself before it goes out.

</details>

<a id="skill-451"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>451. Turn billing texts into a yearly subscription audit</b> —— A year of membership charges — no idea what the total is. Feels like it evaporated.</summary>

A year of membership charges and no idea what the total is. Feels like the money just evaporated.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Collect the year's billing texts as screenshots and send them to Muse.

2. Say: "Tally by app: how much each took this year, and the last charge date."

3. Have it flag the "haven't opened in three months but still charging" ones — decide their fate one by one.

**Real talk:** Collecting screenshots is yours. Canceling is your finger in the app — it just does the math.

</details>

<a id="skill-452"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>452. Lab results + takeout history → a diet fix list</b> —— Checkup full of red arrows, takeout as usual — you know something's gotta change.</summary>

Your checkup is full of red arrows, and the takeout keeps coming. You know something's gotta change — no idea where to start.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your lab report plus last month's takeout order screenshots.

2. Say: "Against the abnormal markers, which of my takeout habits are the worst offenders?"

3. Have it make a "change three things first" list — stick it on the fridge.

**Real talk:** That's lifestyle advice, not medical orders. If the markers are genuinely off, ask a doctor yourself.

</details>

<a id="skill-453"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>453. Let last month's bill set next month's budget</b> —— No idea where the money went monthly — budgets are made and blown.</summary>

No idea where the money went each month. Budgets get made and blown — every month overshoots.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Export last month's statement for Muse — have it sort by food, transport, shopping.

2. Say: "Pick the three worst overspends and set next month's line for each."

3. Weekly, have it reconcile: overspent this week? Ease off for the next few days.

**Real talk:** Exporting the statement is you, from the banking app. It sets the budget — following it is on you.

</details>

<a id="skill-454"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>454. Turn reading notes straight into a thread</b> —— Finished the book, notes everywhere — but you can't shape a decent long post.</summary>

Finished the book with notes everywhere, but you can't shape them into a decent long post to share.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Dump your raw reading notes on Muse.

2. Say: "Pick the three punchiest ideas and expand them into an 8-part thread outline."

3. Fill the outline with your own examples and takes, proofread once, post it.

**Real talk:** It distills ideas fine, but your real takes have to be yours. Let it invent everything and you get correct-sounding nothing.

</details>

<a id="skill-455"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>455. Turn voice memos into an idea bank</b> —— Great idea while driving — you bark it into a memo, then never open it again.</summary>

A great idea hits while you're driving — you bark it into a voice memo, then never open it again.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Transcribe a week's voice memos to text and send them all to Muse.

2. Say: "Sort these fragments — topics, to-dos, stray thoughts — merge the repeats."

3. Every Sunday, have it pick three from the bank and ask if you're acting on any this week.

**Real talk:** Transcribing is on you first. It stores the ideas — acting on them is still you.

</details>

<a id="skill-456"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>456. Compress the morning inbox into a 3-minute brief</b> —— 50 unread at 8am — read them one by one and the morning's gone.</summary>

50 unread emails in the morning. Read them one by one and the morning's gone.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Have Muse connect to your inbox and scan the unread every morning.

2. Say: "Fold away promos and notifications — only three kinds: needs my reply, needs my decision, touches my money."

3. Listen to the brief on your commute — then open the inbox and handle only the flagged few.

**Real talk:** The inbox authorization is yours to tap. It flags the important ones — replying is still you.

</details>

<a id="skill-457"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>457. Price-drop alert + price history: buy or wait?</b> —— Alert says price drop — you buy, then find it's pricier than last month.</summary>

A price-drop alert makes your finger twitch — you buy, then find it's pricier than last month.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse your wishlist — have it log each item and the price you'd actually pay.

2. When an alert lands, say: "Check this one's price trend over the last three months."

3. Have it call it: real low or fake markdown — then you decide.

**Real talk:** It checks public prices — hidden coupons are invisible to it. Tapping buy is still you.

</details>

<a id="skill-458"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>458. Compare contract redlines at a glance</b> —— They sent a "lightly revised" contract — dozens of pages, comparing word by word would blind you.</summary>

They sent a "lightly revised" contract. Dozens of pages — comparing word by word would blind you.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Send Muse both the old and new versions.

2. Say: "Compare clause by clause — list every change: which clause, what it was, what it is now."

3. Have it flag the changes against you — take the list into the negotiation.

**Real talk:** It finds text differences, not legal advice. Before signing anything real, have a lawyer re-check the key clauses yourself.

</details>

<a id="skill-459"></a>

<details>
<summary><b>459. Turn user feedback into release notes</b> —— Hundreds of feedback messages — writing release notes from memory, you miss the big ones.</summary>

Hundreds of feedback messages in the user group. Writing release notes from memory, you miss the important ones and get yelled at.

**3 prompts, copy and use:**

1. Export the recent feedback and send it to Muse.
